<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{contsnadef} We assume that $\mathbf{t}$ is finite and that $(S_u)_{u\in \mathbf{t}}$ is a $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued BRW. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] The \emph{contour exploration} of $\mathbf{t}$ is the sequence of vertices $\smash{(v_k)_{0\leq k\leq 2( \# \mathbf{t} -1)}}$ 
that is recursively defined as follows: \emph{$v_0\! = \!  \varnothing$; $v_{k+1}$ is the first child (relative to birth-order) of $v_k$ that does not belong to $\{ v_0, v_1, \ldots, v_k\}$ if there is one; if not and if furthermore $v_k \! \neq \! \varnothing$, then 
$\smash{v_{k+1}\! = \!  \overleftarrow{v}_{\!\! k}}$; otherwise $\smash{v_{k+1}\! = \!  \varnothing}$ and $\smash{k+1 \! = \!  2(\# \mathbf{t} \! -\! 1)}$, necessarily}. 


\smallskip

\item[$(b)$]  For all $\smash{k\! \in \!  \{ 0, \ldots ,2(\# \mathbf{t} \! -\! 1)\}}$, we set $\smash{C_k (\mathbf{t})\! = \!  |v_k|}$, the height of $v_k$, and we conveniently set 
$\smash{C_{k} (\mathbf{t})\! = \!  0}$ for all integers $\smash{k\! \geq \!  2(\# \mathbf{t}\! -\! 1)}$.  
We next extend $\smash{C_\cdot (\mathbf{t})}$ continuously on $\smash{\mathbb{R}_+}$ by setting for all $\smash{s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+}$: 
$$ C_{s}(\mathbf{t}) \! = \!  C_{\lfloor s\rfloor} (\mathbf{t})+ \{ s\} (C_{\lceil s\rceil} (\mathbf{t}) \! -\! C_{\lfloor s \rfloor} (\mathbf{t})), $$ 
where $\smash{\lceil s \rceil \! = \!  \lfloor s \rfloor +1}$ and $\smash{\{ s \}\! = \!  s\! -\! \lfloor s \rfloor}$. 
The resulting continuous function $\smash{(C_s(\mathbf{t}))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}$ is called the \emph{contour process} of $\mathbf{t}$. 


\smallskip



\item[$(c)$] For all $\smash{k\! \in \!  \{ 0, \ldots ,2(\# \mathbf{t} \! -\! 1)\}}$ and all $\smash{\ell \! \in \!  \mathbb{N}}$, let $\smash{v_k(\ell)\! \in \!  [\![ \varnothing , v_k ]\!]}$ be such that $\smash{|v_k(\ell)|\! = \!  \ell \! \wedge \! |v_k|}$. 
We set $\smash{W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S}, \ell)\! = \!  S_{\! v_k(\ell)}}$. We first fix $k$ and we extend $\smash{W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S}, \cdot )}$ continuously by setting 
$\smash{W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S},r)\!  = \!    W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S},\lfloor r \rfloor ) + \{ r\} \big( W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S},\lceil r \rceil ) \! -\!  W_{\! k} (\mathbf{S},\lfloor r \rfloor ) \big)}$ for all $\smash{r\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+}$. We extend $W_{\! \cdot} (\mathbf{S}, \cdot)$ continuously by setting 
$$ \forall r,s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, \quad W_{\! s} (\mathbf{S}, r) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
  W_{\! \lceil s \rceil }  \big(\mathbf{S} ,  r\! \wedge \! C_{\! s} (t) \big) & \textrm{if $\; |v_{\lceil s \rceil } | \! = \!  1+ |v_{\lfloor s \rfloor }|$,}\\
 W_{\! \lfloor s \rfloor}  \big( \mathbf{S} , r\!  \wedge \! C_{\! s} (t) \big)  & \textrm{if $\; |v_{\lfloor s \rfloor} | \! = \!  1+ |v_{ \lceil s \rceil }|$.}
\end{array} \right. $$
The process $\smash{W_{\! \cdot} (\mathbf{S}, \cdot )}$ is the \emph{snake associated with} $\mathbf{S}$. We also introduce 
$\smash{\widehat{W}_{\! s} (\mathbf{S}) \! = \!   W_{\! s} (\mathbf{S}, C_{\! s} (t))}$, $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, and we call the $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued process 
$\smash{(\widehat{ W}_{\! s} (\mathbf{S}))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+^*}}\! $ the \emph{endpoint process}. $\hfill \square$ 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{heightlukadef} We assume that $\mathbf{t}$ is a finite ordered rooted tree. 

\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] The \emph{depth-first exploration of $\mathbf{t}$} $(u_j)_{0\leq j<\# \mathbf{t}}$ is the contour exploration without repetition: namely, $u_j\! :=\! v_{m_j}$ where $m_j\! = \!  \inf \big\{ k\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}: \# \{ v_0, v_1, \ldots, v_k \} \! = \!  j+1\big\} $. 
It is convenient to set $u_{\# \mathbf{t}} \! = \!  \varnothing$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] For all $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \# \mathbf{t}$, we set $V_{0}(\mathbf{t})\! = \!  0$ and $V_{l+1} (\mathbf{t}) \! = \! V_{l}(\mathbf{t})  + k_{u_{l}} (\mathbf{t})\! -\! 1 $. We extend $V_\cdot (\mathbf{t})$ to $\mathbb{R}_+$ by setting $V_s (\mathbf{t})\! = \!  V_{\lfloor s \rfloor} (\mathbf{t})$ if $s\! \in \!  [0, \# \mathbf{t} +1)$ and $V_{s} (\mathbf{t}) \! = \!  -1$ for all $s\! \in \!  [\# \mathbf{t} +1 , \infty)$. The process $V_\cdot (\mathbf{t})$ is the \emph{\L{}ukasiewicz path} of $\mathbf{t}$.  

\smallskip

\item[$(c)$] For all $0\! \leq \!  l\! \leq \!  \# \mathbf{t}$, we set $H_l(\mathbf{t}) \! = \!  |u_l|$, the height of $u_l$. We extend $H(\mathbf{t})$ continuously on $[0, \#\mathbf{t}]$ as follows: for all integers $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \#\mathbf{t} $ and all $s\! \in \!  [0,1]$, we set 
\end{compactenum}
\begin{equation}
\label{ctrvshght}
\!\! H_{l+s}(\mathbf{t})  \! = \!  \left\{  \begin{array}{ll}
\!\! H_l (\mathbf{t})\! - \! \big(H_l (\mathbf{t}) \! -\! |u_l \! \wedge \! u_{l+1}| \big) ((2s)\! \wedge \! 1) \!  + (2s\! -\! 1 )_+ {\bf 1}_{\{ l\neq \# \mathbf{t}-1\}}  \!\! & \!\!  \textrm{if $|u_{l+1}| \! \leq \! |u_l |$ } \\
\!\! H_l (\mathbf{t}) + s   \!\! &\!\!  \textrm{if  $|u_{l+1}| \! > \!  | u_l | $.}
\end{array} \right.
\end{equation}
\begin{compactenum}
\item[]$H_\cdot (\mathbf{t})$ is the \emph{height process} of $\mathbf{t}$. $\hfill \square$ 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{defLaplclass} We denote by $\mathscr{L}$ the space of critical \emph{L{\'e}vy-Khintchine} Laplace exponents $\psi$, which are of the form:
\begin{equation}
\label{LK}
\forall z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, \quad \psi (z) \! = \!  \beta_\psi z^2 + \int_{\mathbb{R}_+^*} \!\!\!  (e^{-zx} \! -\! 1+ zx)\,  \pi_\psi (dx).
\end{equation}
Here $\smash{\beta_\psi \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+}$ is the \emph{Brownian component} and the \emph{L{\'e}vy measure} $\smash{\pi_\psi} $ satisfies 
the following integrability condition: 
$\smash{\int_{\mathbb{R}^*_+}  ( x \wedge x^2) \pi_\psi (dx) \! < \! \infty}$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{Sheuexplain} We fix $x,c ,\beta \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 
Let $\psi\! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$). Let $\smash{(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, 
$\smash{(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, $\smash{(\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n, u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$, 
$\smash{(\mathcal T_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, and $(C,Y,W)$ be as in $\emph{\textbf{Case (i)}}$, $ \mathbf i \! \in \!  \{ 0,1,2\}$. 
Let $\smash{\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot}$ and $\smash{\mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot}$ 
be as above. We also recall that $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$, $\smash{V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ and 
$\smash{W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ are rescaled as in (\ref{renormCW}) and we recall from (\ref{psidisdef}) the definition of $\smash{\psi_n}$. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)} and 
\emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $)}.

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] We assume that conditionally given $\smash{\mathbf{t}_n}$, the jumps of $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n}$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued i.i.d.~r.v.s with law $\smash{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (dx)\! = \!  \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\beta}}e^{-(|x|\sqrt{2})/\sqrt{\beta}} dx}$. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} implies \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}
and $\smash{b_n/ (a_n (\log  a_n)^2)\! \to \! \infty}$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] In $\emph{\textbf{Case (0)}}$, 
\emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu }$)} 
is \emph{equivalent} to the tightness of the laws of the endpoint processes $\smash{(\widehat{\mathscr W}^{_{(n)}}_\cdot)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}}$. Moreover, it implies that 
$\smash{\lim_{n\to \infty} ( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , \mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}  )\! = \!  ( C_\cdot , C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W_\cdot )}$ in law in $\smash{(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})^2 \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})\times \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})}$. 



\smallskip


\item[$(iii)$]  In $\emph{\textbf{Case (i)}}$, $\mathbf i \! \in \!  \{ 0,1,2\}$, 
\emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu }$)} is \emph{equivalent} to the tightness of the laws of the endpoint processes 
$\smash{(\widehat{W}^{_{(n)}}_\cdot)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}}$. Moreover, it implies that 
$\smash{\lim_{n\to \infty} ( C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}  )}$ $\! = \! $ $\smash{ ( C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, \sqrt{\beta} W_\cdot )}$ in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R}) \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})}$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{heightstuff} As already mentioned, \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) and  \texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$) imply \texttt{Norm}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$) and we recall that \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) implies 
\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) and \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{sibinddef} 
Let $\smash{\mathbf{S}\! = \!  (S_u)_{u\in \mathbf{t}}}$ be a $\smash{\mathbb{R}^d}$-valued 
BRW whose jumps are $\smash{(\xi_u)_{u\in \mathbf{t} \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}}$. We denote by 
$\smash{\mathtt{Lf} (\mathbf{t})\! = \!  \{ u\! \in \!  \mathbf{t}: k_u(\mathbf{t})\! = \!  0\}}$ the set of \emph{leaves} of $\mathbf{t}$ and for all 
$\smash{u\! \in \!  \mathbf{t} \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (\mathbf{t})}$ we set $\smash{\overline{\xi}_u \! = \!  (\xi_{u\ast [j]} )_{1\leq j\leq k_u(\mathbf{t})} }$, 
where $\smash{u\ast [j]}$ stands for the $j$-th child of $u$. We then say that the jumps of $\mathbf{S}$ are 
\emph{independent by group of siblings} if the following holds true. 
\begin{equation}
\label{sibinddef}
\hspace{1mm} \textrm{Conditionally given $\mathbf{t}$, the $\smash{\overline{\xi}_u}$, $\smash{u\! \in \!   \mathbf{t} \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (\mathbf{t})}$, 
are independent. \hspace{10mm} $\hfill \square$ } 
\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{siblindep} $(a)$ Of course independence by group of siblings includes the simpler cases where conditionally 
given $\mathbf{t}$ the jumps $\smash{(\xi_u)_{u\in \mathbf{t} \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}}$ are independent. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ Note that the conditional law of $\smash{\overline{\xi}_u}$ may depend on $\mathbf{t}$ and on $u$, and that the jumps of two siblings 
are not necessarily independent conditionally given $\mathbf{t}$. However, if $\smash{u\! \in \!  \mathtt{Lf} (\mathbf{t})}$, then the r.v.s 
$\smash{\xi_v}$, $\smash{v\! \in\,  ]\!] \varnothing , u ]\!]}$ are independent conditionally given $\mathbf{t}$ (with possibly distinct 
conditional laws). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{iidass}  When the jumps of the $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n}$ are i.i.d., the previous set of assumptions simplifies and becomes  
\textbf{i.i.d.}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \beta}$): 
$\mathbf{(1')}\! =\! \mathbf{(1)}$,  $\mathbf{(6')}\! =\! \mathbf{(6)}$ and 
\begin{compactenum} 

\smallskip


\item[$\mathbf{(2')}$] For all $\smash{n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}}$, $\smash{\mathbf{t}_n}$ is a.s.~finite and conditionally given $\smash{\mathbf{t}_n}$, the jumps of $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n}$ are i.i.d.~r.v.s whose law $\smash{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n}$ is deterministic integrable and centered. 



\smallskip

\item[$\mathbf{(3')}$] $\smash{\sup_{n\in \mathbb{N} } \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} G( \lvert y\rvert ) {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n (dy)\! < \!  \infty}$.

\smallskip


\item[$\mathbf{(4')}$] $\smash{\lVert \beta_n \! -\! \beta \rVert_\infty \! \to \! 0} $ where $\smash{\beta_n}$ is the deterministic matrix  $\smash{\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} y_iy_j {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n (dy) ; 1\! \leq \!  i,j\! \leq \!  d \big)}$.  

\smallskip

\item[$\mathbf{(5')}$] $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} \! \to \! C_\cdot}$ 
in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}} $. $\hfill \square$ 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{extenscv}
 Let $\smash{(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ and $\smash{(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ be two renormalization sequences satisfying \emph{\texttt{Norm}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}}$)}. Let $\smash{(\mathbf{t}_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ be a sequence of a.s.~finite random trees whose rescaled contour processes $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ are as in (\ref{renormCW}). Let $\smash{(C_s)_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}\, $ be a continuous nonnegative process. 
We assume that there are two moment gauge functions $\smash{G_{\! \ast}}$ and $G$, two r.v.s~$\smash{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\! \ast}\!  : \! \Omega \! \to \! \mathbb{R}_+}$ and $\smash{\boldsymbol{\beta}\! : \! \Omega \! \to \! \mathrm{Sym}^{_+}_{^d}}$, and two sequences of BRWs, 
$\smash{\mathbf{S}^\ast_n\! = \!  (S^\ast_{n,u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$ and $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n,u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, that satisfy the following. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] The BRWs $\smash{(\mathbf{S}^{ \ast}_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued and satisfy \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}^\ast_\cdot, G_{\! \ast}, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}_{\! \ast}}$)}. 

\smallskip



\item[$(b)$] ${\bf P}$-.a.s.~$\smash{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\! \ast} \in \mathbb{R}_+^*}$.   




\item[$(c)$] The laws of the endpoint processes $\smash{(\widehat{W}_{\cdot}^{_{\ast,(n)}})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, are tight in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}}$, where $\smash{W_{\cdot}^{_{\ast,(n)}}\!\!}$ stands for the snake of $\smash{\mathbf{S}^\ast_n}$ rescaled as in (\ref{renormCW}). 


\smallskip


\item[$(d)$] The BRWs $\smash{(\mathbf{S}_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ are $\smash{\mathbb{R}^d}$-valued and satisfy \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}}$)}.
\end{compactenum}

\smallskip

\noindent
We denote by $\smash{W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! }$ the rescaled snake of $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n}$ as in (\ref{renormCW}). 
Then, there is a continuous version of a $\smash{\mathbb{R}^d}$-valued Brownian snake $W$ with lifetime process $C$.
and $\smash{(C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot\! ,  W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot) \! \to \! (C \, , \, \sqrt{ \boldsymbol{\beta}}.W )}$  in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})}$.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{maincvsnake} 
We fix $\smash{x, c \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*}$ and $\smash{\alpha \! \in \!  (1, 2]}$. 
Let $\smash{\psi\! \in \!  \mathscr L}$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)}. Let $\smash{(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, 
$\smash{(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, $\smash{(\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$, $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n, u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$ and 
$(C,Y,W)$ be as in any $\emph{\textbf{Case (i)}}$, $ \mathbf i \! \in \!  \{ 1,2,3\}$.
Recall that $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$, $\smash{V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ and $\smash{W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ 
are rescaled as in (\ref{renormCW}). 
We make the following assumptions. 

\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi}$)} and \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu} $)}.
\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] We suppose that there is $\smash{\boldsymbol{\beta}\! : \! \Omega \! \to \! \mathrm{Sym}^{_+}_{^d}}$ and a moment gauge function $G$ as in (\ref{momgaudef}) such that the sequence of BRWs $\smash{(\mathbf{S}_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ satisfies 
\emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}}$)} $(\mathbf{1}$-$\mathbf{5})$.  

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}

\noindent
Then, the following limit holds in law in 
$\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})}$.
\begin{equation}
\label{jcvbis}
\big( C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot\! ,  W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \big)  \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\; }\big( C \, , \,  \sqrt{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.W \big) .
\end{equation}
Furthermore, if $\smash{\lim_{n\to \infty}\big( C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot\! ,  V^{_{(n)}}_\cdot , \boldsymbol{\beta}_n \big) \! = \!   \big( C,V,  \boldsymbol{\beta} \big) }$ holds in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})\times \mathrm{Sym}^{_+}_{^d}}$, then 
(\ref{jcvbis}) holds jointly with $\smash{ V^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! \! \to \! Y}$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{afterthm3}
$(a)$ Let us suppose that the assumptions of Theorem \ref{maincvsnake} hold true in $\textbf{Case (1)}$ for all $\smash{x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*}$ with $\smash{\boldsymbol{\beta}}$ and $G$ independent of $x$. Then (\ref{jcvbis}) holds in $\textbf{Case (0)}$ by standard arguments, and if in $\textbf{Case (1)}$, $\smash{\lim_{n\to \infty}\big( C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot\! ,  V^{_{(n)}}_\cdot , \boldsymbol{\beta}_n \big) \! = \!   \big( C,V,  \boldsymbol{\beta} \big) }$ holds in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})\times \mathrm{Sym}^{_+}_{^d}}$, for all $\smash{x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*}$, then (\ref{jcvbis}) holds jointly with $ \smash{V^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! \! \to \! Y}$ in $\textbf{Case (0)}$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ As mentioned right after (\ref{cvcodgen}), in $\textbf{Cases (1)}$, Assumptions 
\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) alone, and in $\textbf{Cases (2)}$, 
\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) combined with (\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $) $+$ \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)) (which are implied by 
\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $) by Lemma \ref{controlpsi})) actually imply Assumption $(\mathbf{6})$ in \textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}$). This obviously holds true in $\textbf{Case (3)}$ too. 


\smallskip

\noindent
$(c)$ Let us mention that Lemma \ref{sheustable} shows that \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) in $\textbf{Case (3)}$ implies \texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $). 


\smallskip

\noindent
$(d)$ Recall that there is a time-change $\smash{\phi_{n}}$, which allows to derive discrete height processes and height-snakes from  contour processes and snakes: namely, $\smash{H^{_{(n)}}_{\! s} \! = \!  C^{_{(n)}}_{^{\! \phi_n(s)}}}$ and $\smash{\mathcal{W}^{_{(n)}}_{\! s} \! = \!  W^{_{(n)}}_{^{\! \phi_n(s)}}}$, for all $\smash{s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+}$. Since $\smash{\phi_n\! \to \! 2\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{R}_+} }$ 
 in probability in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}}$ (see (\ref{controphit}) for more details), then (\ref{jcvbis}) holds in law jointly with 
 $\smash{(H^{_{(n)}}_\cdot\! , \mathcal{W}^{_{(n)}}_\cdot) \! \! \to \! ((C_{\! 2s})_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+} , (W_{\! 2s})_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+})}$. 



\smallskip
 
\noindent 
$(e)$ In $\textbf{Case (3)}$
and when the jumps of the BRWs are $\mathbb{R}$-valued independent r.v.s with a fixed deterministic law ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$, Theorem \ref{maincvsnake} has been obtained by Janson \& Marckert \cite{JanMar05} when $\alpha\! = \!  2$ and by Marzouk \cite{Mar20} in the other stable cases, with a necessary and sufficient condition in terms of the tail of ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$ (see Section \ref{assumpsec} for a more precise discussion on this point). 
We actually do not provide an independent proof of this result: we only extend it thanks to Theorem \ref{extenscv}. 



\smallskip
 
\noindent 
$(f)$ Let $\psi \! \in \!  \mathscr{L}$ satisfy \texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$) (and thus \texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)). Let the 
$a_n$, $b_n$, $\mu_n$, $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n, u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$ and $(C,Y,W)$ be as in $\textbf{Case (0)}$.
We assume \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) and we assume that 
conditionally given $\mathbf{t}_n$, the jumps of $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n}$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued and i.i.d.~r.v.s whose deterministic law $\smash{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n}$ 
satisfies the following: there are $\smash{c, \beta\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*}$ such that for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{cenunii}
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n ([-c,c]) \! = \!  1, \quad 
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\!  y {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy)\! = \!  0 \quad \textrm{and} \quad \beta_n \! :=\! \int_{\mathbb{R}}\! y^2 {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy) \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\; } \beta.
\end{equation}
Then, $\smash{\sup_{n\in \mathbb{N} } \int_{\mathbb{R}} G( \lvert y\rvert ) {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n (dy)\! < \!  \infty}$ with $\smash{G(x)\! = \!  \exp (|x|\, )}$ and the condition \texttt{Growth}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, G}$) holds iff $\smash{b_n /(a_n  (\log a_n)^4) \! \to \! \infty}$. 
In this case the BRWs $\smash{(\mathbf{S}_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ satisfy \textbf{i.i.d.}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \beta}$) and Theorem \ref{maincvsnake} implies the convergence (\ref{jcvbis}) as noticed in the above Remark \ref{afterthm3} $(a)$. 
However, let us mention that we can prove actually the following slightly better result. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] Under the assumptions \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$), (\ref{cenunii}) and \texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) only, the convergence $\smash{( C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}  )}$  $\to $ $\smash{( C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, \sqrt{\beta} W_\cdot )}$ holds in law in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})\times \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})}$. 


\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] If $\smash{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n \! = \!  \frac{1}{2} (\delta_{-1} \! + \delta_{1})}$, for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ , 
and $\smash{\liminf_{n} \mu_n (1) \! > \!  0}$, then \texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu}$) is equivalent to the tightness in $\smash{\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}}$ of the laws of the endpoint processes $\smash{(\widehat{W}^{_{(n)}}_\cdot)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
This result is stated more precisely in Theorem \ref{unifbounded}, in Appendix 
\ref{unifboundedsec}. Its proof does not rely on the coupling result stated in Theorem \ref{brwcouplingth} and it is rather long. 
As already mentioned, this statement is better than the result obtained by applying Theorem \ref{maincvsnake}. Indeed, Proposition \ref{excntrex} below shows that for each $\psi$ as above, there are 
$a_n, b_n, \mu_n$ such that \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$) and \texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) hold true but $\smash{\limsup_{n\to \infty} b_n /(a_n  (\log a_n)^4)\! < \!  \infty}$: thus, Theorem \ref{maincvsnake} does not apply in these cases.  $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{excntrex} Let $\psi, \phi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)} and \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\phi$)}. 
Suppose that $\beta_\phi \! \leq \!  \beta_\psi$ and let $q \! \in \!  (0, 1)$. Let $\mathbf a \! = \!  (a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a fixed sequence of positive real numbers such that $\lim_{n\to \infty} a_n \! = \!  \infty$. 
Below $ \mathbf b\! = \!  (b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ stands for a sequence of positive real numbers 
such that $ \lim_{n\to \infty}b_n\! = \!  \infty$ 
and $\boldsymbol \mu \! = \!  (\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ stands for a sequence of offspring distributions 
that satisfies (\ref{nontricri}). Then, the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] If \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)} and \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\phi$)} hold true, then there are 
$\mathbf b$ and $\boldsymbol \mu$ satisfying  \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}, $\lim_{n\to \infty} \mu_n (1) \! = \!  q$, $b_n \! \asymp_\infty\!  \phi(a_n)$ and \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}.

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] If \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)} and \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\phi$)} hold true but not \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\phi$)}, then there are $\mathbf b$ and $\boldsymbol \mu$ satisfying  \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}, $\lim_{n\to \infty} \mu_n (1) \! = \!  q$, $b_n \! \asymp_\infty\!  \phi(a_n)$, \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) } but not \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}.


\smallskip



\item[$(iii)$] If \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\phi$) does not hold, 
then there are $\mathbf b$ and $\boldsymbol \mu$ that satisfy  \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}, $\lim_{n\to \infty}$ $ \mu_n (1) \! = \!  q$, $b_n \! \asymp_\infty\!  \phi(a_n)$ but not  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{applicactus} We keep the notations and assumptions of Theorem \ref{maincvsnake}, $\mathbf{Case}(2)$. Let $(\Upsilon_{\! n,u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}$ be a $\mathbb{T}_{\! \mathtt b}$-valued BRW whose law is given by (\ref{lawBRW}). Recall from (\ref{Rbndef}), its range $\mathcal R_{n}$ and its occupation measure $\mathbf m_{{\mathtt{occ}}}^{_{(n)}}$, and recall that $(T_{C,|W|}, d_{C, |W|}, r_{C, |W|}, \mu_{C,|W| })$ stands for the \emph{reflected Brownian cactus with branching mechanism $\psi$}. Then 
 the following limit holds weakly on $(\mathbb M, \delta_{\mathtt{GHP}})$: 
 \begin{equation}\label{cvcactus} \big( \mathcal R_{ n} , \tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_n}} d_{\mathtt{gr}} ,  \mathtt o, \tfrac{1}{b_n}\mathbf m^{(n)}_{\mathtt{occ}}\big) \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;} \big( T_{\! C, |W| }, d_{C, |W|}, r_{ C, |W|}, \mu_{ C, |W|} \big) 
\end{equation} 
jointly with (\ref{cvtolev}).
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{Ulamtree} $(a)$ A \textit{rooted ordered tree} is a subset $  t\! \subset \! \mathbb{U}$ satisfying the following conditions.
\begin{compactenum}
\item[($a_1$)] $\varnothing \! \in \!  t$.
\item[($a_2$)] If $u\! \in \!   t\backslash \{ \varnothing\}$ 
then $\overleftarrow{u}\! \in \!   t$. 
\item[($a_3$)] For all $u\! \in \!   t$, there exists $k_u ( t)\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}\cup \! \{ \infty\}$ such that: $u \! \ast \!  [j]\! \in \!   t$  iff  $1\! \leq \! j \! \leq \! k_u ( t)$. 
\end{compactenum}
Here, $k_u ( t)$ is interpreted as the \textit{number of children of $u$} and if 
$1\! \leq \! j \! \leq \! k_u ( t)$, then $u\ast\!  [j] $ is the \emph{$j$-th child of $u$.} If $k_u(t)\! = \!  0$, $u$ is called a \emph{leaf} of $t$ and we denote by $\mathtt{Lf} (t)$ the set of leaves of $t$. We denote by $\mathbb{T}$ the set of rooted ordered trees. 

\smallskip


\noindent
$(b)$ Let $ t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ and $u\! \in \!  t$. Then 
$\theta_u  t\!  = \! \{ v\! \in \!  \mathbb{U}: u\ast v\! \in \!   t \}\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ is the \emph{subtree of $ t$ stemming from $u$}. 


\smallskip



\noindent
$(c)$ A \emph{forest} is a sequence of trees 
$t\! = \!  (t(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$, $N\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^{*}\! \cup \!\{ \infty\}$. It is sometimes convenient to view it as a single tree $t'\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ with $k_\varnothing(t')\! = \!  N$ such that  $t(p)\! = \!  \theta_{[p]}t'$ for all $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{contord} Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$. Recall from Definition \ref{contsnadef} $(a)$ its contour exploration $(v_k)_{0\leq k < 2(\# t-1)}$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(a) $ Let $(u_l)_{0\leq l<\# t}$ be the $t$-valued sequence that is recursively defined as follows:  
we set $u_0\! = \!  \varnothing$; if $l+1 \! < \!  \#t$, then $u_{l+1}$ is the $\leq_{\mathtt{lex}}$-least element of the (necessarily nonempty) set $\{ u\! \in \!  t \! : \! u_l \! <_{\mathtt{lex}} \! u\}$. 
We call $(u_l)_{0\leq l<\# t}$ the \emph{depth-first exploration of $t$}, which corresponds to Definition \ref{heightlukadef} $(a)$. 

\smallskip



\noindent $(b)$ In general the depth-first and contour explorations may not visit the whole tree. In this article we shall restrict to trees $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ such that 
$\# \theta_{[j]} t\! < \!  \infty$ for all $1\! \leq \!  j\! \leq \!  k_\varnothing (t)$ (with  possibly $k_\varnothing (t)\! = \!  \infty$). Then for such trees we get $t\! = \!  \{ u_l; 0\! \leq \!  j\! < \!  \# t\}\! = \!   \{ v_k; 0\! \leq \!  k\! < \!  2(\#t \! -\! 1)\}$ 
(the tree associated with a forest of finite trees satisfies this condition for instance). 
In this case, the contour exploration crosses each edge of $t$
exactly twice (upwards first and then downwards). Namely, for all $u\! \in \!  t\backslash \{ \varnothing\}$, there is 
a unique pair of integers $k,k^\prime$ such that  
$k\! < \!  k^\prime$ and $(\overleftarrow{u},u)\! = \!  (v_k, v_{k+1})\! = \!  (v_{k^\prime+1} , v_{k^\prime})$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{forestdef} Let $t\! = \!  (t(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$ be a forest of \emph{finite} trees. Let 
$S\! = \!  (S(p)\! = \!  (S_u(p))_{u\in t(p)})_{1\leq p\leq N}$ be $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued BRWs starting at the origin, $N\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^* \cup \{ \infty\}$. 
We define the contour process $(C_{\! s} (t))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, 
the height process $(H_{\! s} (t))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, the \L{}ukasiewicz path $(V_{\! s} (t))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ of the forest $t$ 
and the snake $(W_{\! s} (S, r))_{r,s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ of $S$ in the following way: we set $\mathtt{r}_{0} \! = \!  0$ and $\mathtt{r}_p\! = \!  \sum_{1\leq q\leq p} \# t(q) $, $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$; 
for all $s\! \in \!  [\mathtt{r}_{p-1}, \mathtt{r}_p]$, we set 
$$C_{\!2s}(t)\! = \!  C_{\!2(s-\mathtt{r}_{p-1})} (t (p)), \quad  H_{\! s}(t)\! = \!  H_{\!s-\mathtt{r}_{p-1}} \! (t (p)), \quad V_{\! s}(t)\! = \!  V_{\!s-\mathtt{r}_{p-1}} \!(t (p))\! -\! p +1, 
$$
and for all $r\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, we set $W_{\! 2s}(S, r)\! = \!  W_{\! 2(s-\mathtt{r}_{p-1})} (S (p), r)$. If 
$N\! < \!  \infty$, then for all $s\! \in \!  [\mathtt{r}_N, \infty)$ we also set $C_{\! s } (t)$ $\! = \! $  $H_{\! s} (t)\! = \!  0$, $V_{\! s}(t)\! = \!  -N$ and $W_{\! s} (S,r)\! = \!  0$, $r\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{contord} Let $t\! = \!  (t(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$ and $S\! = \!  \big(S(p)\! = \!  (S_u(p))_{u\in t(p)}\big)_{1\leq p\leq N}$ be as 
above. Let $t'\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be the tree associated to the forest $t$ 
as in Definition \ref{Ulamtree} $(c)$. We define $S'\! = \!  (S'_u)_{u\in t'}$ by setting 
$S'_\varnothing\! = \!  0$ and for all $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$, 
$S'_{[p]\ast u} \! = \!  S_u(p)$, $u\! \in \!  t(p)$. 
In what follows, we denote by $(v_k)_{0\leq k \leq 2(\# t'-1)}$ and $(u_l)_{0\leq l < \#t'}$ the contour exploration and the depth-first exploration of $t'$. 

\medskip

\noindent
$(a)$ The respective encoding processes of $(t, S)$ and $(t', S')$ are closely related. Namely, 
observe for all $s,r\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$ that 
$$C_s (t)\! = \!  (C_{s+1} (t') -1)_+  \quad \textrm{and} \quad W_{\! s} (S, r)= W_{\! s+1} (S', r+1), $$
and for all integers $0\! \leq \!  l \! < \!   \# t'$, $H_l (t)\! = \!  (H_{l+1} (t') -1)_+$ and 
$V_{l+1} (t)\! = \!  V_l (t)+ k_{u_{l+1}} (t) \! -\! 1$. Therefore results on encoding processes of forests can be derived from similar results on trees. 

 
\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ Contour and height processes are closely related. To simplify, let us assume first that $N\! = \!  \infty$. 
We observe that 
\begin{equation}
\label{Klcontour}
\forall l\! \in \!  \mathbb{N},  \quad K_l:= 2l \! -\! H_{l} (t) \! = \!  \inf \{ k\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}: v_{k+1}\! = \!  u_{l+1}\}
\end{equation}
which increases with $l$. Thus for all $l\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, 
$C_{K_l} (t) \! = \!  H_{l} (t)$ and for all $s\! \in \!  [K_l, K_{l+1} ]$, we get
\begin{equation}
\label{ctrvshght_prime}
C_s (t) = \left\{  \begin{array}{ll}
(H_{l}(t) \! -\! s +K_l)_+ &  \textrm{if $s\! \in \!  [K_l, K_{l+1} \! -\! 1)$ } \\
(H_{l+1}(t) \! -\! K_{l+1} +s )_+ & \textrm{if $s\! \in \!  [K_{l+1} \! -\! 1, K_{l+1}] $.}
 \end{array} \right.
\end{equation}
See D.~\& Le Gall \cite{DuLG02}, Section 2.4, for more details. 
We can derive $H_\cdot (t)$ from $C_\cdot (t)$ by a time-change $\phi_t \! : \!\mathbb{R}_+  \! \to \! \mathbb{R}_+ $ that is defined as follows: 
we set $\phi_t(0)\! = \!  0$ and for all $l\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and all $s\! \in \!  [0, 1]$, we set $\phi_t(l+s)\! = \!  K_l+s$ if $|u_{l+1}| \! > \!  |u_l|$ and otherwise we set 
$$\phi_t(l+s) \! = \!   K_l + (K_{l+1} \! -\! 1 \! -\!  K_l ) 
((2s)\! \wedge \! 1) + (2s\! -\! 1)_+ \! \! = \!    2(l+s) \! -\! H_l(t)+ (H_l(t)\! -\! H_{l+1} (t)) ((2s)\! \wedge \! 1).$$
Then, $\phi_t$ is an increasing continuous function such that 
\begin{equation}
\label{controphit}
\forall s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+ , \forall l\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*, \quad H_s (t)\! = \!  C_{\! \phi_t(s)} (t) \quad \textrm{and} \quad \max_{r\in [0, l]} \big| \tfrac{1}{2} \phi_t(r) \! -\! r\big| \leq \tfrac{1}{2} \!\!\!\!\! \sup_{\quad r\in [0, l] }  \!\!\!\!\!H_r (t) \; .
\end{equation}
When the number $N$ of trees in the forest $t$ is finite, the height process $H_\cdot (t)$ is also obtained from $C_\cdot (t)$ by a continuous increasing time-change
$\phi_t \! : \! \mathbb{R}_+\! \to \! \mathbb{R}_+$ and (\ref{controphit}) holds true too.  $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{Contlifespan} Let $T\! = \!  (t, (\ell_u)_{u\in t})$ be as above. 
Let $(v_k)_{0\leq k\leq 2 (\# t-1)}$ and $(u_l)_{0\leq l< \# t }$ be resp.~the contour and the depth-first explorations of $t$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(a)$ We define $(\Lambda_T(k))_{0\leq k\leq 2\#t}$ by 
$$  \Lambda_T(0)\! = \!  0, \; \Lambda_T(1)\! = \!  \zeta_{v_0} \quad \textrm{and} \quad  \Lambda_T (k+1)\! = \!  \Lambda_T (k)+ |\zeta_{v_k} \!\! -\! \zeta_{v_{k-1}}|, \quad  1\! \leq \!  k \! \leq \!  2(\#t \! -\! 1),  $$   
and $\Lambda_T (2\# t)\! = \!  \Lambda_T (2\# t\! -\! 1) + \ell_\varnothing$, if $\#t \! < \!  \infty$.


\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ We define $(L_T (l))_{0\leq l\leq \#t}$ by $L_T(0)\! = \!  0$ and $L_T (l)\! = \!  \sum_{0\leq j< l} \ell_{u_j}$, for all $1\! \leq \!  l\! \leq \!  \# t$. 
We also set $\mathtt r'\! = \!  L_T(\# t)$. Note that $\Lambda_T(2\#t)\! = \!  2 \mathtt r '$. 


\smallskip

\noindent


\noindent
$(c)$ The \emph{contour of $T$} is the unique 
continuous function $(\mathscr C_s(T))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+\! }$ that satisfies the following conditions: 
$\mathscr C_0 (T)\! = \!  0$; $\mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(k)} (T)\! = \!  \zeta_{v_{k-1}}$, for all $1\! \leq \!  k\! < \!  2\#t $, and 
$\mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(2\# t)} (T)\! = \!  0$; $\mathscr C_\cdot (T)$ is affine on $[\Lambda_T (k), \Lambda_T(k+1)]$, for all $0\! \leq \!  k\! < \! 2 \# t$;  $\mathscr C_{s} (T)\! = \!  0$ for all $s\! \in \!  [2\mathtt r'\! , \infty)$. 





\smallskip



\noindent
$(d)$ The \emph{height process of $T$} is the unique c{\`a}dl{\`a}g function $(\mathscr H_s(T))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+\! }$ that satisfies the following conditions: $\mathscr H_0 (T)\! = \!  0$; $\mathscr H_{L_T (l) +s} (T)\! = \!  \zeta^*_{u_l} +s$, for all $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \# t $ and $s\! \in \!  [0, \ell_{u_l})$; $\mathscr H_s (T)\! = \!  0$, for all $s\! \in \!  [ \mathtt r' \! , \infty)$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{lifespanforest} Let $\smash{T\! = \!  \big( T(p)\! = \!  (t(p), (\ell_u(p))_{u\in t(p)} ) \big)_{1\leq p\leq N}}$ be a forest of finite $\mathbb{R}_+$-marked trees, $N$ being possibly infinite.
The contour and height processes of the foret $T$, denoted by 
$(\mathscr C_s(T))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ and $(\mathscr H_s(T))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, are obtained by concatenation of the processes 
$\mathscr C_\cdot (T(p))$ and $\mathscr H_\cdot (T(p))$ and similarly for $\Lambda_T $ and $L_T $.  
Namely, we define $\Lambda_T(0)\! = \!  L_T(0)\! = \!  \mathtt r_0\! = \!  \mathtt{r}'_{0} \! = \!  0$ and 
for all $1\! \leq \!  p \! \leq \!  N$, $\smash{\mathtt r_p\! = \!  \sum_{1\leq q\leq p} \# t(q)}$, $\smash{\mathtt{r}'_p\! = \!  \sum_{1\leq q\leq p} \sum_{u\in \mathbf{t}(q)} \ell_u(q)}$, 
$$ \forall s\! \in \!  [\mathtt{r}'_{p-1}, \mathtt{r}'_p], \quad \mathscr C_{2s}(T)\! = \!  \mathscr C_{2s-2\mathtt{r}'_{p-1}}\!  (T(p)) \quad \textrm{and} \quad \mathscr H_{\!s}(T)\! = \!  \mathscr H_{\!s-\mathtt{r}'_{p-1}}\!  (T(p)), $$ 
and $\mathscr C_{2s}(T)\! = \!  \mathscr H_{\!s}\! = \!  0$, for all $s\! \in \!  [\mathtt r'_N, \infty)$ 
(with an obvious convention if $N\! = \!  \infty$ and 
$\mathtt r_N'\! = \!  \infty$). Similarly, we set $\Lambda_T (k+ 2\mathtt r_{p-1}) \! = \!  \Lambda_T(2\mathtt r_{p-1} )+ \Lambda_{T(p)} (k)$ for all $0\! \leq \!  k\! \leq \!  2\# t(p)$, 
and $L_T (l+ \mathtt r_{p-1}) \! = \!  L_T(\mathtt r_{p-1} )+ L_{T(p)} (l)$ for all $0\! \leq \!  l\! \leq \!  \# t(p)$ and 
$\Lambda_T$ and $L_T$ are constant on resp.~$[2\mathtt r_N, \infty)$ and $[\mathtt r_N, \infty)$ (with an obvious convention if $N\! = \!  \infty$ and thus $\mathtt r_N\! = \!  \infty$). 
Note that $\Lambda_T(2\mathtt r_p)\! = \!  2L_T(\mathtt r_p)\! = \!  2\mathtt r_p'$$\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{contlifespanrem} Let $\smash{T\! = \!  \big( T(p)\! = \!  (t(p), (\ell_u(p))_{u\in t(p)} ) \big)_{1\leq p\leq N}}$ be as in Definition \ref{lifespanforest} and let $t'\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be the tree associated with the forest $t\! = \!  (t(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$ 
as in Definition \ref{Ulamtree}. We define $T'\! = \!  (t', (\ell'_u)_{u\in t'})$ similarly by setting 
$$\ell'_\varnothing \! =\!  0, \quad \ell'_{[p]\ast u}  \! =\! \ell_u(p), \; 1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N, \, u\! \in \!  t(p), \quad \textrm{and} \quad \zeta'_u = \zeta'^*_u+ \ell'_{u}= \!\!  \!   \sum_{v\in [\![ \varnothing, u]\!] }\!\!    \ell'_v, \;\,  u\! \in \!  t' \backslash \{ \varnothing\}. $$
In what follows, we denote by $(v_k)_{0\leq k \leq 2(\# t'-1)}$ and $(u_l)_{0\leq l < \#t'}$ the contour and depth-first explorations of $t'$. 
When $t'$ is finite (which happens iff $N$ is finite), we observe that $\# t'\! -\! 1\! = \!  \mathtt r_N$. In this case, it is convenient to set $u_{\# t'}\! = \!  \varnothing$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(a)$ Let $\Lambda_T$ be as in Definition \ref{lifespanforest}. We first observe that $\Lambda_T (k+1)\! -\! \Lambda_T(k) \! = \!  |\zeta'_{v_{k+1}}\!\!\! -\! \zeta'_{v_k}|$, for all $0\! \leq \!  k \! < \!  2\mathtt r_N$. 
We extend $\Lambda_T$ continuously on $\mathbb{R}_+$ 
by setting $\Lambda_T (s)\! = \!  
\Lambda_T(\lfloor s \rfloor ) + \{ s\} (\Lambda_T(\lceil s \rceil )\! -\! \Lambda_T(\lfloor s \rfloor ))$ for $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$.  
We easily check that 
\begin{equation}
\label{timechangecontourlifespan}
\forall s \! \in \! [0, 2\mathtt r_N) \quad \mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(s)} (T)\! = \!  \zeta'_{v_{\lfloor s \rfloor }} + 
\{ s\} (\zeta'_{v_{\lceil s \rceil }}\! -\! \zeta'_{v_{\lfloor s \rfloor }} ) .
\end{equation}
In the next sections, the lifespans are most of the time i.i.d.~$\texttt{expo} (1)$ r.v.s, so that $C (t)$ and 
$\mathscr C(T)$ are close. More precisely, for all $0\! \leq \!  k \! \leq \!  2\mathtt r_N$, 
(\ref{timechangecontourlifespan}) entails
\begin{equation}
\label{compCTCt1}
\max_{r\in [0, k]} \big| \mathscr C_{\Lambda_T (r)} (T)\! -\! C_{r}(t) \big| \leq 2+
 \max \Big\{ \big| \zeta'_{v_i} \! \! -\! 1\! -\! (|v_i|\! -\! 1)_+ \big|\,  ; \,  1\! \leq \!  i\! \leq \!  k  \Big\} .
\end{equation}
\emph{Indeed}, we recall from Remark \ref{contord} $(a)$ that $C_k(t)\! = \!  (C_{k+1} (t')\! -\! 1)_+\! = \!  (|v_{k+1}| \! -\! 1)_+$ and 
$|C_{r} \! -\! C_{(r-1)_+} (t)| \! \leq \!  1$, for all $r\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. 


\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ We next want to compare $\Lambda_T $ with $2L_T$. To that end, we first note 
that 
\begin{equation}
\label{LTident} L_T(l)\! = \!  \sum_{0\leq j\leq l} \ell'_{u_j} , \quad 0\! \leq \!  l\! \leq \!  \mathtt r_N\! = \!  \# t'\! -\! 1.
\end{equation}
We extend $L_T$ continuously on $\mathbb{R}_+$ 
by setting $L_T (s)\! = \!  
L_T(\lfloor s \rfloor ) + \{ s\} (L_T(\lceil s \rceil )\! -\! L_T(\lfloor s \rfloor ))$, $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. 
We recall $K_l$ from (\ref{Klcontour}) and $\phi_t$ from Remark \ref{contord} $(b)$. For all $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \mathtt r_N$, by definition of $K_l$, $v_{1+K_l}\! = \!  u_{l+1}$ and $\smash{v_{K_l}\! = \!  \overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}$. Then, we check that 
$\smash{\Lambda_T (1+\phi_t(l))\! = \! \Lambda_T (1+K_l)\! = \!  2L_T(l+1) \! -\! \zeta'_{u_{l+1}}}$.
Thus $\smash{\Lambda_T (1+K_l)\! -\!  \Lambda_T (K_l)\! = \!  
|\zeta'_{u_{l+1}}\!\!\! -\! \zeta'_{\overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}|\! = \!  \ell'_{u_{l+1}}\! = \!  L_T(l+1)\! -\! L_T(l)}$. 
Therefore we get 
\begin{equation}
\label{basicconstat}
\Lambda_T (K_l)\! = \!  \Lambda_T (\phi_t(l)) \! = \!   2L_T(l) \! -\! (\zeta'_{u_{l+1}} \! \! \! -\ell'_{u_{l+1}})= 2L_T(l)\! -\! \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}} . 
\end{equation} 
Since $L_T$ and $\Lambda_T \circ \phi_t $ are nondecreasing, we get 
\begin{eqnarray*} 
\forall s\! \in \!  [l, l+1], \quad \zeta'_{u_{l+2}}\!\!\! -\! \ell'_{u_{l+2}} \!\! \! \!\! - 2\ell'_{u_{l+1}} \! \!\!= 2L_T(l)  \!\!\! \!\! &- & \!\!\! \!\!\Lambda_T(\phi_t(l\! +\! 1))\leq  2L_T(s)\! -\! \Lambda_T(\phi_t(s))\\
\!\!\! & \! \leq \!  & \!\!\! 2L_T(l\! +\! 1)\! -\! \Lambda_T(\phi_t(l)) \! = \!  \ell'_{u_{l+1}}\!\! + \zeta'_{u_{l+1}} .
\end{eqnarray*}
Namely, $\smash{\max_{s\in [0, l]}|2L_T(s)\! -\! \Lambda_T(\phi_t(s))| \! \leq \!  2\max_{1\leq j\leq l}\zeta'_{u_{j}}}$. 
By definition of $K_j$ and by (\ref{timechangecontourlifespan}), we get 
$\smash{ \zeta'_{u_{j+1}}\! = \!  \zeta'_{v_{1+K_j}}\! = \!  \mathscr C_{\Lambda_T (1+K_j)} (T)}$. Since $\smash{\Lambda_T (1+K_j) \! \leq \!  2L_T (j+1)}$, we get for all $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \mathtt r_N$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{controlLambda}
\max_{s\in [0, l]} \big|2L_T(s)\! -\!  \Lambda_T (\phi_t(s))  \big| \leq 2\!\!\!\!\!\! \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!  \max_{\quad \quad s\in [0, 2L_T(l)]} \!\!\!\!\!\! \!\!\!\!\!\! \mathscr C_s(T) .
\end{equation}
This extends to all $l\in \mathbb{N}$, since $ \Lambda_T $ and $2L_T$ are constant and both equal to $2\mathtt r'_N$  on resp.~$[2\mathtt r_N, \infty)$ and $[\mathtt r_N, \infty)$. 


\smallskip

\noindent
$(c)$ We view $\smash{\mathscr C(T)}$ as a time-change of $\smash{\mathscr H(T)}$. As already noticed, 
$\smash{v_{1+K_l}\! = \!  u_{l+1}}$ and $\smash{v_{K_l}\! = \!  \overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}$. By (\ref{timechangecontourlifespan}), we then get 
$\smash{\mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(K_l)}(T)\! = \!  \zeta'_{v_{K_l}} \!\! \! = \!  \zeta'_{ \overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}  \!\!\!\!\! = \!  \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}}}$. We also see that 
\begin{equation}
\label{cHindetof}
\mathscr H_{L_T(l)+s}\! = \!  \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}} \!\!\!+s, \quad s\! \in \!  [0, \ell'_{u_{l+1}})\! = \!  [0, L_T(l+1)\! -\! L_T(l)).
\end{equation}
By definition, $\smash{\mathscr C(T)}$ is affine on 
$\smash{[\Lambda_T(K_l), \Lambda_T(1+K_l)]}$ and, as already noticed, $\smash{\mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(1+K_l)}(T) }$ $ \! = \! $ $ \smash{\zeta'_{u_{l+1}}\! = \!  \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}}+ \ell'_{u_{l+1}}}$ and $\smash{\zeta'_{v_{1+K_l}} \!\!\!\! -\! \zeta'_{v_{K_l}} \! = \!  \ell'_{u_{l+1}}}$. 
Thus $\smash{\Lambda_T(1+K_l)\! = \!  \Lambda_T(K_l)+ \ell'_{u_{l+1}}}$, and we get $\smash{\mathscr C_{\Lambda_T(K_l)+s}(T)\! = \!  \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}}\!\! +s\! = \! \mathscr H_{L_T(l)+s}} $, for all $\smash{s\! \in \!  [0, \ell'_{u_{l+1}})}$. 
For all $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \mathtt r_N$, and for all 
$s\! \in \!  [L_T(l), L_T(l+1))$, we set 
\begin{equation} 
\label{defcapK}\mathcal K_T(s)\! = \!  s\! -\! L_T(l)+\Lambda_T(K_l)
\end{equation}
 and for all $s\! \in \!  [\mathtt r_N, \infty)$, we set $\mathcal K_T(s) \! = \!  2s$. Then, we have proved that 
\begin{equation}
\label{ccCvsccH}
\forall s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, \quad \mathscr C_{\mathcal K_T(s)} (T)= \mathscr H_s(T).  
\end{equation} 
We now compare $\mathcal K_T(s)$ to $2s$. 
Let $l \! < \!  \mathtt r_N$ and $s\! \in \!  [L_T(l), L_T(l+1))$. We note that $\smash{2s\! -\! \mathcal K_T(s)}$ $\! = \! $ $\smash{s\! -\! L_T(l)+ 2L_T(l)\! -\! \Lambda_T(K_l)\! = \!   s\! -\! L_T(l)+ \zeta'^*_{u_{l+1}}\!\! \! \in \!  [0,  \zeta'_{u_{l+1}}]}$, by (\ref{basicconstat}). 
Since $\smash{\zeta'_{u_{l+1}}\!\!\! \! = \!  \mathscr H_{L_T(l+1)-}(T)} $ $\! = \! $ $\smash{ \mathscr C_{\Lambda_T (1+K_l)}}$ and since 
$\Lambda_T (1+K_l)$ $ \! \leq \!  $ $2L_T(l+1)$, we get 
\begin{equation}
\label{Kvs2id}
\forall l\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*, \quad \max_{s\in [0, L_T(l)]} \big| 2s \! -\! \mathcal K_T(s) \big| \leq \max_{s\in [0, L_T(l)]} \mathscr H_s(T) \leq \max_{s\in [0, 2L_T(l)]}  \mathscr C_s(T)  \, , 
\end{equation} 
which provides a sufficient control of $\mathcal K_T$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(d)$ We next check that 
\begin{equation}
\label{capKlowupbounds}
\forall s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, \quad s\! \leq \!  \mathcal K_T (s) \! \leq \!  2s \; .
\end{equation} 
\emph{Indeed}, let $0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \mathtt r_N$ and $s\! \in \!  [L_T(l), L_T(l+1))$. By (\ref{basicconstat}) and (\ref{defcapK}), 
$\smash{\mathcal K_T(s)\! -s\! \! = \!  } $ $\smash{\Lambda_T(K_l)\! -\! L_T(l)\! = \!  }$ $\smash{ L_T(l) \! -\! \zeta'^{\ast}_{u_{l+1}}}$. Then, 
we use (\ref{LTident}) to see that $\smash{L_T(l) \! = \!  \sum_{u\leq_{\mathtt{lex}} u_l} \ell'_u}$. 
By definition $\smash{\zeta^{'\ast}_{u_{l+1}}\! = \!  \zeta'_{\overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}}$. Since 
$\smash{\overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1} \! \leq_{\mathtt{lex}}\!  u_l}$, 
we get $\smash{\sum_{u\leq_{\mathtt{lex}} u_l} \ell'_u \! \geq \!  \zeta'_{\overleftarrow{u}_{\! l+1}}}$. 
This proves $s\! \leq \!   \mathcal K_T (s)$. Right after (\ref{ccCvsccH}), we show that 
$\smash{2s\! -\! \mathcal K_T(s) \! \in \!  [0,  \zeta'_{u_{l+1}}]}$, which proves  $\mathcal K_T (s) \! \leq \!  2s $. 
If $s\! \geq \!  \mathtt r_N$, $\mathcal K_T(s)\! = \!  2s$ and (\ref{capKlowupbounds}) is trivially satisfied.  $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
\label{randmarktreedef} $\!\! (a)$ We denote by $\mathscr F(\mathbb{T})$ the sigma-field on $\mathbb{T}$ generated by the subsets 
$\{ t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}\!  : \! v\! \in \!  t\}$, where $v$ ranges in $\mathbb{U}$. A random tree is a $(\mathscr F, \mathscr F(\mathbb{T}))$-measurable function $\tau\! : \! \Omega \! \to \! \mathbb{T}$. 

\smallskip




\noindent
$(b)$ Let $(E,d)$ be a metric space equipped with its Borel sigma field $\mathscr B(E)$. 
On the space  $\mathbb{T}_{\! E}\! :=\!  \{ (t, (s_u)_{u\in t})\, ; \, t \! \in \!  \mathbb{T}, \, s_u\! \in \!  E, u\! \in \!  t \}$ of \emph{$E$-marked trees}, we denote by 
$\mathscr F(\mathbb{T}_{\! E})$ the sigma field generated by the sets $\{ (t, (s_u)_{u\in t}) \! \in \!  \mathbb{T}_{\! E}\! : \! v\! \in \!  t \; \textrm{and} \; s_v\! \in \!  B \}$, where $v$ ranges in $\mathbb{U}$ and $B$ ranges in $\mathscr B(E)$. A random $E$-marked tree is a $(\mathscr F, \mathscr F(\mathbb{T}_{\! E}))$-measurable function 
$\mathcal T\! = \!  (\tau, (s_u)_{u\in \tau} ) \! : \! \Omega \! \to \! \mathbb{T}_{\! E}$. 
Although a $d$-dimensional BRW $(\tau, (S_u)_{u\in \tau})$ is formally a 
$(\mathscr F, \mathscr F(\mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{R}^d}))$-measurable r.v.~we simply denote it 
by $S\! = \!  (S_u)_{u\in \tau}$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
\label{GWfordef} Let $\mu\! = \!  (\mu(k))_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$ be a probability measure on $\mathbb{N}$ and let $q\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(a)$ A \textit{Galton--Watson tree with offspring distribution $\mu$} (a \textit{GW($\mu$)-tree}, for short) is a $(\mathscr F, \mathscr F(\mathbb{T}))$-measurable r.v.~$\tau\! : \! \Omega \!\to \! \mathbb{T}$ which satisfies the following. 
\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(i)$] $k_\varnothing (\tau)$ has law $\mu$. 
\item[$(ii)$] For all $k\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ such that $\mu(k)\! >\! 0$, the subtrees $\theta_{[1]} \tau, \ldots , \theta_{[k]} \tau$ under ${\bf P} (\, \cdot \, | k_\varnothing (\tau)\! = \! k)$ are independent with the same law as $\tau$ under ${\bf P}$. 
\end{compactenum}


\smallskip


\noindent
$(b)$ A \emph{GW($\mu$)-tree with independent and exponentially distributed lifespans with parameter $q$} (a \emph{GW($\mu, q$)-tree} for short) is a $(\mathscr F, \mathscr F(\mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{R}_+}))$-measurable 
r.v.~$\mathcal T\! = \!  (\tau, (\ell_u)_{u\in \tau}) \! : \! \Omega \!\to \! \mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{R}_+}$ such that $\tau$ is a GW($\mu$)-tree and conditionally given $\tau$, the r.v.s $\ell_u$, $u\! \in \!  \tau$, are independent and exponentially distributed with parameter $q$. 

\smallskip


\noindent
$(c)$  A \emph{GW($\mu$)-forest} (resp.~a \emph{GW($\mu,q$)-forest}) is a (possibly infinite) sequence $\tau\! = \!  (\tau (p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$ (resp.~$\mathcal T\! = \!  (\mathcal T(p)\! = \!  (\tau (p), (\ell_u(p))_{u\in \tau(p)} ))_{1\leq p\leq N}$) such that conditionally given $N$, the $\tau(p)$ (resp.~the $\mathcal T(p)$), $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$ are i.i.d.~GW($\mu$)-trees (resp.~ i.i.d.~GW($\mu,q$)-trees). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
\label{snadef} Let $h\! : \! \mathbb{R}_+ \! \to \! \mathbb{R}_+$ be lower semicontinous, and for all $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, let
$w_s(\cdot) \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}}$. Then $(h,w)$ is a $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued \emph{snake} if the following holds. 
\begin{compactenum}


\smallskip


\item[$(a)$] For all $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$ and for all $r\! \in \!  [h(s), \infty)$, $w_s (r)\! = \! w_s (h(s))\! =: \! \widehat{w}_s $. 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] For all $s_1, s_2 \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, 
$w_{s_1} (r)\! = \! w_{s_2} (r)$ for all $r\! \in \!  [0,m_h (s_1, s_2)]$ where we have set $m_h (s_1, s_2)\!   =\!  \inf_{s\in [s_1\wedge s_2, s_1\vee s_2]} h(s)$. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
\noindent
The snake $(h,w)$ is said continuous if $h$ is continuous and if the function $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+ \! \mapsto \! w_s (\cdot) \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}}$ is continuous. We denote by $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$ the \emph{space of $d$-dimensional continuous snakes}. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{easy} 
$(a)$ For all $f,f'\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}}$, we set  
$\delta^{{\mathtt{unif}}}_{^{\! d}} (f,f')\! = \!  \sum_{p\in \mathbb{N}^*} 2^{-p} (1\wedge \max_{s\in [0, p]} |f(s)\! -\! f'(s)|)$. Then, the space 
$(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}} , \delta^{\mathtt{unif}}_{^{\! d}} )$ is Polish and $\delta^{\mathtt{unif}}_{^{\! d}} $ metrizes the topology of uniform convergence on every compact intervals. For all $(h,w)$, $(h',w') \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \!  \mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}})$, we next set 
\begin{equation}
\label{snaspacedist}
D_d \big(  (h,w), (h',w')\big)\! := \! \delta^{\mathtt{unif}}_{{1}} (h,h') + \sum_{p\in \mathbb{N}^*} 2^{-p}\!\! \! \sup_{s\in [0, p]} 
\delta^{\mathtt{unif}}_{{\! d}} \big(w_s (\cdot), w_s'(\cdot)\big).
\end{equation}
Then $(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \!  \mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}}), D_d)$ is a Polish metric space yielding the product topology of 
$(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! 1}}, \delta^{\mathtt{unif}}_1)$ with $\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}})$ equipped 
with the topology of uniform convergence on every compact intervals.  
We easily check that $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$ is closed in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \!  \mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}})$, equipped with the product topology, and $(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d, D_d)$ is thus Polish.  

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ If $(h,w)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$, then note that $\widehat{w}\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$ and that $(h,w)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d \! \mapsto \! \widehat{w}\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$ is continuous. 



\smallskip

\noindent
$(c)$ Let $(h,w)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$ and let $s, r\! \in \!  [0, \infty)$. There is a measurable function 
$G_{r,s}$ from $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}\! \times \! (\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})^2$ to $\mathbb{R}^d$ such that 
$\smash{G_{r,s} (h,w_0, \widehat{w})\! = \!  w_{s}(r)}$. \emph{Indeed}, for any function $h'\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$ we set 
$\alpha_{r,s}(h')\! = \!  \sup \{ s^\prime\! \in \!  [0, s]: h'(s^\prime)\! < \!  r\wedge h'(s)\}$ with the convention that $\sup \emptyset\! = \!  0$. Observe that for all $s'\! \in \!  (0, s)$, $\alpha_{r,s}(h')\! > \!  s'$ iff $m_{h'}(s',s)\! < \!   r\wedge h'(s)$, which implies that $h' \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}\mapsto \alpha_{r,s} (h')\! \in \!  [0, s]$ is measurable. Then we take 
$\smash{G_{r,s} ( h,w_0, \widehat{w})\! = \!  w_0(r)}$ if $r \! < \!  m_h(0, s)$ and 
$\smash{G_{r,s} ( h,w_0, \widehat{w})\! = \!  \widehat{w}_{\alpha_{r,s} (h)}}$ otherwise; $G_{r,s}$ is clearly measurable 
and the snake property then entails the desired result. 

Now recall that the Borel sigma-field of $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$ is generated by the functions $f\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d} \!  \mapsto\!  f(r)\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}^d$, with $r$ ranging in $\mathbb{R}_+$. Similarly the  Borel sigma-field of $\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}})$ is generated by the functions $w\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^{\! d}}) \! \mapsto \! w_s (\cdot) \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$, with $s$ ranging in $\mathbb{R}_+$. 
This combined with the previous result implies that there is a measurable 
function $G$ from $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}\! \times \! (\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})^2$ to $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$ such that $\smash{G(h,w_0, \widehat{w})\! = \!  (h, w)}$ for all $(h,w)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$. 


\smallskip

\noindent
$(d)$ Let $(S_u)_{u\in t}$ be a $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued BRW and let $W_\cdot (S, \cdot)$ stand for its discrete snake as in Definition \ref{contsnadef}. Then, we observe that $(C_\cdot (t), W_\cdot (S, \cdot))\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$. A similar result holds for the height snake. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{endmargcar} Let $(C, W)$ and $(C ', W')$ be two $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$-valued r.v.s such that a.s.~$C_0\! = \!  C_0'\! = \!  0$. 
We assume for any $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ and any real numbers $0\! \leq \!  s_1\! \leq \!  \ldots \! \leq \!  s_p$ that $\smash{(C_{\!s_j}; \widehat{W}_{\! s_j})_{1\leq j\leq p}}$ has the same law as 
$\smash{(C'_{\!s_j}; \widehat{W}'_{\! s_j})_{1\leq j\leq p}}$. Then $(C,W)$ has the same law as $(C',W')$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{endsnake1} Let $(h,w)$ be a $d$-dimensional snake. Then, the following holds true.
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] We suppose that $h\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$ and that 
$\widehat{w}\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$. Then, $(h,w)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$. 
Moreover, $\smash{\omega (w, \eta, [0, N])} $ $\! \leq \!  $ $\smash{2 \omega (\widehat{w}, \eta, [0, N])}$ for all $\eta\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and all $N\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$.

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] Let $(h,w)$ and $\smash{(h^{(n)}\! , w^{(n)})\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, be such that 
$\smash{\lim_{n\rightarrow \infty} h^{(n)}\! = \!  h}$ in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$, 
$\smash{\lim_{n\rightarrow \infty} w_{^0}^{_{(n)}}}$ $\! = \! $ $\smash{ w_0}$ and $\smash{\lim_{n\rightarrow \infty} \widehat{w}^{{(n)}}\! = \!  \widehat{w}}$ in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d}$. Then $\smash{\lim_{n\rightarrow \infty} w^{(n)}\! = \!  w}$ in $\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
\label{Brosnadef} 
A $d$-dimensional Brownian snake is a pair of processes $(C, W)$ such that $C$ is $\mathbb{R}_+$-valued and lower semicontinuous, $W$ is a snake with lifetime $C$ as in Definition \ref{snadef} and for all $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$, and all real numbers 
$0\! \leq \!  s_1\! < \!  \ldots \! < \!  s_p$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{margBrosna}
\big(W_0,W_{\! s_1}, \ldots , W_{\! s_p} \big) \; \overset{\textrm{(law)}}{=} \;  \mathtt{Marg}_p \big( C \, , (B^{_{(j)}}_{\cdot}) _{0\leq j\leq p}  , (s_j)_{1\leq j\leq p} \big)\; , 
\end{equation}
where $\smash{B^{_{(0)}}_{\cdot} \!\! \! = \!  W_{\! 0} }$ and where the $\smash{(B^{_{(j)}}_{\cdot})_{1\leq j\leq p}}$ are independent standard $d$-dimensional Brownian motions with initial value $0$ that are independent from $W_0$ and $C$. We say that $(C,W)$ is continuous if, in addition, a.s.~$(C,W)\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_d$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{Bsnarem} The family of laws provided by the right hand side 
of (\ref{margBrosna}) actually forms 
a consistent family of finite dimensional marginal laws (so that the previous definition makes sense). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{BrosnapsiH} Let $\psi\! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Grey}$(\psi)$}. Let $(H_s)_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ be a continuous $\psi$-height process as defined in (\ref{defH}). We set $C_s \! = \!  H_{s/2} $, $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip


\item[$\!\! \!\! (i)\! $] $\! C$ is the lifetime-process of a continuous $d$-dimensional Brownian snake iff $\psi$  satisfies 
\emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{\,}$($\psi$)}.

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)\!\!$] We assume \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{\,}$($\psi$)} and we denote by $W$ a continuous $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process $C$. Let $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We recall from (\ref{infireach}) the definition of $\varsigma_{-x}$. Then for all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{exitsys}
{\bf P} \Big( \! \! \! \! \! \! \max_{\quad s\in [0, 2\varsigma_{-x} ]} \! \! \! \! \! \!  \widehat{W}_{\! s} \! \leq \!  z  \Big)= e^{-xw(z)} \quad \textrm{where} \quad \int_{w(z)}^\infty \frac{ds}{2\sqrt{\int_0^s \psi (r) dr}}= z \; . 
\end{equation}
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{osc1lemma} We keep the above notations. We assume that $(h,w)$ is a $\mathbb{R}$-valued \emph{continous} snake. Let $s_0, s_1 \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$ be such that $s_0 \! < \!  s_1$ and let $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We suppose the following. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] For all $r\! \in \!  [ m_h (s_0,s_1), h(s_0)]$, $ \big| \widehat{w}_{s_0} \! -\! w_{s_0} (r)  \big| \! \leq \!  z$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] $\max_{s\in [s_0, s_1]} \big| \widehat{w}_{s} \! -\! \widehat{w}_{s_0}\big| \! > \!  7z$. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
Then there exists $q\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ such that $s_0 \! < \!  {\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\! q} (w,z) \! < \!   {\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\! q+1}(w,z) \! < \!   s_1$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{fungeneprop} $f_\mu \! : \! [0, 1] \! \rightarrow \!  [0, 1\! -\! \mu (0)]$ is a strictly concave increasing bijection and  $g_\mu\!  : \! [0, 1]\!  \rightarrow \! [\mu (0), 1]$ and $ \Psi_{\! \mu} \! : \! [0, 1] \! \rightarrow \! [0, \mu (0)]$ are 
strictly convex increasing bijections.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{hghtestimm} Let $\mu$ satisfy (\ref{nontricri}). Let $\tau$ be a GW($\mu$)-tree. 
There is a universal constant $c_0\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ such that for all $s\! \in \! \mathbb{R}_+$
\begin{equation}
\label{hghtestii}
\int_{\! c_0{\bf P}(\Gamma(\tau) >s)}^1\!\!  \frac{\mathrm dr}{\, \Psi_{\! \mu} (r)} \, \geq  \tfrac{1}{2}s \quad \textrm{and if $\mu(1) \! > \!  0$, \quad } \int_{{\bf P} (\Gamma (\tau) > s)}^1 \!\!  \frac{\mathrm dr}{\, \Psi_{\! \mu} (r)} \, \leq (s + 1) \frac{\log \frac{1}{\mu(1)}}{1\! -\! \mu(1)} \; .
 \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
\label{1brosnadistree} We keep the above notations and we denote by $(\mathscr W_{s} (T,r))_{r,s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ 
the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with initial value $0$ and lifetime process $\mathscr C_\cdot(T)$. We also set 
\begin{equation}
\label{defviatimechange}\mathtt W_s(T,r)= \mathscr W_{\mathcal K_T(s)} (T,r), \quad r,s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, 
\end{equation}
which we call the  \emph{$1$-dimensional height Brownian snake} with initial value $0$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{constrheightBrosna}
$(a)$ Note that $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+\! \mapsto \!  \mathtt W_s(T, \cdot) \! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$ is c{\`a}dl{\`a}g and that actually 
it is distributed as a $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with initial value $0$ and lifetime process $\mathscr H_\cdot(T)$. Indeed, $\mathscr H_\cdot (T)$ is c{\`a}dl{\`a}g without positive jump, hence it is lower semicontinuous, as required in Definition \ref{Brosnadef} of Brownian snakes. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ We shall need a more direct construction of $1$-dimensional height Brownian snake of $T$. To that end, we denote by $T'\! = \!  (t', (\ell'_u)_{u\in t'})$ the $\mathbb{R}_+$-marked tree associated with the forest $T$ as explained in Remark \ref{contlifespanrem} and Definition \ref{Ulamtree} $(c)$. Namely, for all $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$, $\theta_{[p]} t'\! = \!  t(p)$, $\ell'_{[p]\ast u} \! :=\!  \ell_u(p)$, for all $u\! \in \!  t(p)$ and $\ell'_\varnothing\! = \!  0$. We recall that the death-time of $u\! \in \!  t'$ is $\zeta'_u\! = \!  \sum_{v\in [\![ \varnothing, u]\!]} \ell'_v$ and its birth-time is denoted by $\zeta'^*_u\! = \!  \zeta'_u \! -\! \ell'_u$. We denote by $(u_l)_{0\leq l\leq \# t'\! -\! 1}$ the sequence of the vertices of $t'$ listed in increasing depth-first order, i.e., lexicographical order. We recall the process $(L_T (l))_{l\in \mathbb{N}}$ 
from Definitions \ref{Contlifespan} and \ref{lifespanforest}. We recall from (\ref{LTident}) 
that $ \# t'\! -\! 1\! = \!  \mathtt r_N\! = \!  \sum_{1\leq p\leq N} \# t(p)$ and that for all $0\! \leq \!  l\! \leq \!  \mathtt r_N$,  
$L_T (t) \! = \!  \sum_{0\leq k\leq l} \ell'_{u_j}$. 
We recall from (\ref{cHindetof}) that 
$\mathscr H_{L_T(l) +s} (T)\! = \!  \zeta_{u_{l+1}}'^*\!\!\! +s$, for all $0\! \leq \!  l \! < \!  \mathtt r_N$ and $s\! \in \!  [0 ,\ell'_{u_{l+1}})$.   

By definition of $\mathtt W_{\cdot} (T,\cdot)$, the paths 
$s\! \in \!  [0, \ell'_{u_{l+1}}) \mapsto \widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l) +s}(T) \! -\! \widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l)} (T)$ are independent 
stopped Brownian motions starting at $0$. Note that the left-limit of $\smash{\widehat{\mathtt{W}}_\cdot }$ 
at $\smash{\ell'_{u_{l+1}}}$ exits and is equal to 
$\smash{\widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l+1)-}(T)}$.  
We now extend these Brownian paths thanks to other independent Brownian motions as follows.  
Let $(B'_s(u))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, $u\! \in \!  \mathbb{U}$, be a family of auxiliary i.i.d.~$1$-dimensional Brownian motions with initial value $0$, which are furthermore supposed to be independent from $\mathtt W_\cdot( T, \cdot)$. For all 
$0\! \leq \!  l\! < \!  \mathtt r_N$ and all $s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, we set 
\begin{equation}
\label{pathudef}
B_s(u_{l+1})= \left\{ \begin{array}{ll}
\widehat{\mathtt W}_{ L_T(l)+s} (T) \! -\! \widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l)} (T)  & \textrm{if $s \! < \!  \ell'_{u_{l+1}}$,} \\
\widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l+1)-}(T)  \! -\! \widehat{\mathtt W}_{L_T(l)} (T)  + B'_{s-\ell'_{u_{l+1}}} (u_{l+1})& \textrm{if $s \! \geq \!  \ell'_{u_{l+1}}$,}
  \end{array} \right.
\end{equation} 
We also set $B_\cdot (\varnothing)\! = \!  B'_\cdot (\varnothing)$, which plays no role in what follows.   
This defines the paths $(B_s(u))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ for all $u\! \in \!  t'$. 
We recall from Definition \ref{randmarktreedef} that $\smash{ \mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{R}_+\times \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}} }$ stands for the space of $\smash{(\mathbb{R}_+\times \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})}$-marked ordered rooted discrete trees  and we observe the following. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] The $(B_s(u))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, $u\! \in \!  t'$, are i.i.d.~$1$-dimensional Brownian motions with initial value $0$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] There is a measurable function $F\! : \! \mathbb{T}_{\mathbb{R}_+\times \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}} \! \to \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+,  \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$ such that 
\begin{equation}
\label{iidpathssnaconstr}
 F \big( \Theta \big)  = \big( \mathtt W_s(T, \cdot) \big)_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+} .
\end{equation} 
where we have set $\Theta\! = \!   \big( t', (\ell'_u, B_\cdot (u) )_{u\in t'} \big) $.  $\hfill \square$ 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{compareBrowsnakeBRW} 
We keep the previous notation and we assume that $((\ell_u(p))_{u\in t(p)})_{1\leq p\leq N}$ are 
independent exponentially distributed r.v.s with mean $1$. Let $\mathbf S\! = \!  
((S_u(p) )_{u\in t(p)})_{1\leq p\leq N}$ be the BRW whose jumps are given by 
$\smash{\xi_u(p)\! :=\! B_{\ell'_{{[p]  \ast  u} }}\!  ([p ]  \ast  u)}$, $u\! \in \!  t(p)\backslash 
\{ \varnothing\}$, $1\! \leq \!  p\! \leq \!  N$. We recall that $\smash{(\widehat{W}_{\! s}(\mathbf S))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}$ 
is the endpoint process of the snake of $\mathbf S$ as in Definition \ref{contsnadef}, and that $\smash{(\widehat{\mathscr W}_{\! s} (T))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}$ is the endpoint process of the contour-Brownian 
snake of $T$ as in Definition \ref{1brosnadistree}. Then, the following holds true. 


\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] The jumps $((\xi_u(p))_{u\in t(p)\backslash \{ \varnothing\}})_{1\leq p\leq N}$ are i.i.d.~with law ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (dx)\! = \!  \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} e^{-|x|\sqrt{2}} dx$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] Let $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ such that $p\! \leq \!  N$. For all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{closeWWexpli}
{\bf P} \Big( \max_{s\in [0, 2\mathtt r_p]} \big|\widehat{W}_{\! s}(\mathbf S) \! -\! \widehat{\mathscr W}_{\Lambda_T (s)} (T) \big|\! > \!  z\sqrt{2}\Big)\leq 1-\Big( 1-2e^{-z/3}\Big)^{\! \mathtt r_p} . 
\end{equation}


\smallskip

\item[$(iii)$] For all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, 
$ 1\!-\! \big( 1\! -\!  e^{-z\sqrt{2}} \big)^{\mathtt r_p -p}  \leq  2{\bf P} \big( \max_{s\in [0, 2\mathtt r_p]} 
\widehat{W}_{\! s} (\mathbf S) \! > \!  \tfrac{1}{2}z\big) \; .$ 

\smallskip

\item[$(iv)$] For all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, 
$ 1\!-\! \big( 1\! -\!  e^{-z\sqrt{2}} \big)^{\mathtt r_p}  \leq  2{\bf P} \big( \max_{s\in [0, \Lambda_T (2\mathtt r_p)]} \widehat{\mathscr W}_s (T) \! > \!  \tfrac{1}{2} z\big) \; .$ 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{maxBrosnadiscr} Let $\smash{\mathcal T\! = \!  (\tau, (\ell_u)_{u\in \tau})}$ be a GW$(\mu, q)$-tree as in Definition \ref{GWfordef} 
$(b)$. To simplify notation we set $\smash{\mathscr W\! = \!  \mathscr W_\cdot (\mathcal T, \cdot) }$, $\smash{\mathtt W\! = \!  \mathtt W_\cdot (\mathcal T, \cdot) }$ and $\smash{\mathscr Z_\cdot \! = \!  \mathscr Z_\cdot  (\mathcal T)}$. 
For all $r\! \in [0, 1]$ and all $a\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, we also set 
$\smash{w_r(a)\! = \!  1\! -\! {\bf E} [r^{\mathscr{Z}_a}]}$ and $\smash{w(a) \! = \!  \lim_{r\to 0^+} w_r(a)\! = \!  
 {\bf P} (\max_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+} \!\!  \widehat{\mathscr W}_{ s}  \! \geq \!  a)}$. Then, the following holds.  
 \begin{equation}
\label{exitBroSna}
\int_{w_r(a)}^{1-r}\frac{dy}{2\sqrt{\int_0^y \Psi_{\! \mu} (z) dz}}= \int_{w(a)}^{1} \frac{dy}{2\sqrt{\int_0^y \Psi_{\! \mu} (z) dz}}= a\sqrt{q}. 
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{renewsnake} We keep the above notations. Let $\mathbf s$ be a $(\mathscr F_{\! s})_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$-stopping time. We assume that $\mathbf s$ is a.s.~finite. Then $\mathtt W^{_{[\mathbf s]}}_{\cdot} (\cdot)$ is independent from $\mathscr F_{\! \mathbf s}$ and it has the same distribution as $\mathtt W_\cdot (\cdot)$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{renewosci} We keep the previous notations and assumptions. Then a.s.~for all $q\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, $\mathbf s_q (z) \! < \!  \infty$ and the r.v.s $(\mathbf s_{q+1} (z) \! -\! \mathbf s_q (z))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ are i.i.d.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{recallSakhanenko}
Let $G$ be a moment gauge function as in (\ref{momgaudef}). Let $ K_0, y \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 
Let $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}\backslash\{ 0, 1\}$
and let $(\xi_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}$ be $\mathbb{R}$-valued r.v.s. We assume that the probability space $(\Omega, \mathscr F\! , {\bf P})$ is sufficiently rich to carry an additional r.v.~$U$ that is uniformly distributed on $[0, 1]$ and that is independent of $(\xi_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}$. We assume the following. 

\smallskip

\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(a)$] The r.v.s $(\xi_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}$ are independent.

\smallskip


\item[$(b)$] For all $j\! \in \!  \{ 1, \ldots , n\}$,  ${\bf E}[ G(\xi_j)] \! \leq \!  K_0 \sigma_j^2\! < \!  \infty$, where $\sigma^2_j\! = \!  {\bf E}[ \xi_j^2]$, and ${\bf E}[ \xi_j]\! = \!  0$. 

\smallskip


\item[$(c)$] $y^{-2} G(y) \! \geq \!  K_0$. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}

\noindent
Then, there is $K_1\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ which only depends on $K_0$ and $G$, and there are 
independent standard Gaussian r.v.s $(Y_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}$, which 
depend measurably on $K_0, G, (\xi_j)_{1\leq j\leq n}, U$ and $y$, such that 
$$ {\bf P} \Big(\max_{1\leq k\leq n} 
\Big|\! \sum_{1\leq j\leq k} \!\!  \xi_j  \! -\! \sigma_jY_j \,  \Big| \! >\!  K_1y \Big) \leq \frac{4K_0 (\sigma_1^2+ \ldots + \sigma_n^2)}{G(y)} .$$
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
\label{kappax0} In the definition (\ref{momgaudef}) of the moment gauge function $G$, two positive parameters $\kappa$ and $x_0$ are involved and are part of the very definition of $G$. Therefore in the previous statement and in the next statements, when we mention that a quantity depends on $G$ we actually mean that it depends on $G$, $\kappa$ and $x_0$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
\label{HSnumber} ({\small \textbf{Horton-Strahler number}}) 
Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be a finite tree. For any function $\varphi \! : \! t \! \rightarrow \! \mathbb{N}$ and for all
 $u\! \in \!  t \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (t)$, we set $\overline{\varphi} (u)\! = \!  \max\{ \varphi (v); v\! \in \!  t: \overleftarrow{v}\! = \!  u \}$. 
Then the \emph{Horton-Strahler flow} is the $\mathbb{N}$-valued function $\phi$ on $t$ that is characterised by the following. 

\smallskip

\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(a)$] For all $v\! \in \!  \mathtt{Lf} (t)$, $\phi (v)\! = \!  1$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] For all $u\! \in \!  t \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (t)$, $ \phi (u)= \overline{\phi} (u) + {\bf 1}_{\{ \exists v, v^\prime \in t\; : \;  v\neq v^\prime, \;  \overleftarrow{v}= \overleftarrow{v}^\prime= u  \; \textrm{and} \;  \phi (v)= \phi (v^\prime) =  \overline{\phi} (u)\}}. $
\end{compactenum} 

\smallskip

\noindent
The \emph{Horton-Strahler number} of $t$ is then defined as $\mathtt{HS} (t)\! : = \! \phi (\varnothing)$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{brwcouplingth} Let $G$ be a moment gauge function as in (\ref{momgaudef}). Let $ C_1, C_2, C_3, y \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 
Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be finite. Its Horton-Strahler number is denoted by $\mathtt{HS} (t)$ and its set of leaves by $\mathtt{Lf} (t)$. Let $(\xi_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ be $\mathbb{R}$-valued r.v.s. 
We assume that the probability space $(\Omega, \mathscr F\! , {\bf P})$ is sufficiently rich to carry an additional r.v.~$U$ that is uniformly distributed on $[0, 1]$ and that is independent of $(\xi_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$. We assume the following. 

\smallskip

\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(a)$] The r.v.s $\big( (\xi_{[u]\ast i} )_{1\leq i\leq k_u (t)}; u \! \in t \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (t)\big)$ are independent.

\smallskip


\item[$(b)$] For all $u\! \in \!  t\backslash \{ \varnothing \} $, $\;  {\bf E} \big[ G(\xi_u) \big] \! \leq \!  C_1$, $\; C_2 \! \leq \! \sigma_u^2\! := \!  {\bf E} \big[ \xi_u^2\big] \! \leq \!  C_3$ and $\; {\bf E} [ \xi_u] \! = \!  0$. 


\smallskip


\item[$(c)$]  $ \big( \frac{y}{\mathtt{HS} (t)} \big)^{\! -2} G\big( \frac{y}{\mathtt{HS} (t)}\big)\! \geq \!  C_1/C_2$.

\smallskip


\end{compactenum}

\noindent
Then, there exists $K\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and independent standard Gaussian r.v.s $(Y_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ such that 
\begin{equation}
\label{coupling1}
 {\bf P} \Big(\max_{u\in t} \big|S_u \! -\! R_u \big| \! >\!  Ky \Big) \leq 2C_1\!  \Big(1+ \frac{2C_3}{C_2} \Big) \, \frac{\# t}{G  \big(\frac{y }{\mathtt{HS}(t)}  \big)} 
 \end{equation}
 where for all $u\! \in \!  t\backslash \{ \varnothing \}$, we have set 
$S_u \! = \!  \sum_{v\in ]\!] \varnothing , u ]\!]} \xi_v$, $\, R_u \! = \!  \sum_{v\in ]\!] \varnothing , u ]\!]} \sigma_vY_v$ and $S_\varnothing\! = \!   R_\varnothing\! = \!   0$. 
Here, $K$ depends on $C_1$, $C_2$, $C_3$ and $G$ only; the r.v.s
$(Y_u)_{u\in t\backslash \{ \varnothing\} }$ depend measurably on $C_1$, $C_2$, $C_3$, $G$, $(\xi_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$, $U$ and $y$.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{HSloppconn}  We keep the previous notations. Then 
\begin{equation}
\label{connectHS}
\mathtt{HS} (t)= \min M (\mathbf{r})  \leq 1+ \log_2 \big(\# \mathtt{Lf} (t) \big) \; , 
\end{equation}
where the minimum is taken over all the possible rankings $\mathbf{r}$ of the leaves of $t$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{Gaussjumps}
Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be finite.
We denote by $\Gamma (t)\! = \!  \max_{u\in t} |u|$ its \emph{total height}. 
Let $(Y_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ be independent $\mathbb{R}$-valued standard Gaussian r.v.s. Let 
$\sigma$ and $(\sigma_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ belong to $\mathbb{R}_+^*$. We set 
$$ \delta \! = \!  \max_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}} |\sigma^2_u \! -\! \sigma^2| , \quad 
\, R_u =\!  \sum_{v\in ]\!] \varnothing , u ]\!]} \sigma_vY_v \, , \quad    
R^\bullet_u  = \! \sum_{v\in ]\!] \varnothing , u ]\!]} Y_v, \quad u\! \in \!  t \backslash \{ \varnothing\} $$
and $R_\varnothing\! = \!  R^\bullet_\varnothing \! = \!  0$. To avoid trivialities, we assume that $\delta\! >\! 0$. Then, for all $z_1, z_2\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$,
\begin{equation}
\label{Gausscontrol}
{\bf P} \Big( \! \max_{u\in t} \big| R_u\! -\! \sigma R^\bullet_u \big| \! >\!  z_1+z_2 \Big)\leq 4 \, {\bf P} \Big( \! \max_{u\in t} \big| R^\bullet_u \big| \! >\!  \sigma z_1/\delta\Big)+ 4 \exp\!  \left( \! \! -\frac{z_2^2\sigma^2}{ 2\, \delta^2  \Gamma (t)}  
 \right) .
\end{equation}
If we furthermore assume that $\sigma^2  \! > \!  \delta$, then 
\begin{equation}
\label{Gausscontrol2}
{\bf P} \Big( \! \max_{u\in t} \big| R^\bullet_u \big| \! >\!  z_1+z_2 \Big)\leq 4 \, {\bf P} \Big( \! \max_{u\in t} \big| R_u \big| \! >\! z_1\sqrt{\sigma^2 \! -\! \delta} \Big)+ 4 \exp\!  \left( \! \! -\frac{z_2^2 (\sigma^2 \! -\! \delta)}{ 2\, (\sigma^2 \! +\! \delta)  \Gamma (t)}  
 \right). 
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{fdestimate} 
Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be finite. Let $\kappa \! \in \!  (0, 1)$ and let $G$ be a moment gauge function such that 
$G(x) \! \geq \!  |x|^{2+ \kappa}$ for all $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}$. 
Let $(\xi_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ be $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued r.v.s~that satisfy the following.

\smallskip

\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(a)$] The r.v.s~$\big( (\xi_{[u]\ast i} )_{1\leq i\leq k_u (t)}; u \! \in t \backslash \mathtt{Lf} (t)\big)$ are independent.

\smallskip


\item[$(b)$] $M \! :=\! \max_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}} {\bf E} \big[ G(|\xi_u|)  \big] \! < \!  \infty$ 
and ${\bf E} [ \xi_u] \! = \!  0\, $ for all $u\! \in \!  t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}$. 

\smallskip


\end{compactenum}
Let $(Y_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$  be independent $d$-dimensional 
Gaussian random vectors whose covariance matrix is the identity. Let $\beta\! = \!  (\beta_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j \leq d} \! \in \!   \mathrm{Sym}^{_+}_{^d}$.
We denote by $(S_u)_{u\in t}$ and 
$(R_u)_{u\in t}$ the BRWs whose jumps are resp.~$(\xi_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ and $(Y_u)_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}}$ and whose initial value is $S_\varnothing \! = \!  R_\varnothing \! = \! 0$. We set $\lVert \beta \rVert$ $ \!= \!$ $ \max_{1\leq i,j\leq d} |\beta_{i,j}|$, $ m_1 \! = \!  \max_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}} {\bf E} \big[ |\xi_u| \big]$, 
and  
$$ \delta \! = \!  \max_{u\in t \backslash \{ \varnothing\}} \max_{1\leq i,j\leq d}\big| {\bf E} \big[ \langle \mathtt{e}_i , \xi_u \rangle  \langle \mathtt{e}_j , \xi_u \rangle \big] \! -\! \beta_{i,j}\big| \; .$$
Let $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$, $u_1, \ldots, u_p \! \in \!  t \backslash \{ \varnothing \}$ and $y_1, \ldots, y_p\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}^d$. We set $r\! = \!  \max_{1\leq j\leq p} |y_j| $. Then, 
\begin{eqnarray}
\label{fdcontrol}
\Big| {\bf E} \Big[ \exp \!\! \!\! \!\! \!\! & & \!\! \!\! \!\! \!\! \Big( \!\! \!\! \sum_{\; \; 1\leq k\leq p}   \mathrm{i}\,  \langle y_k , S_{u_k} \rangle   \Big) \Big] \!
- \! {\bf E} \Big[ \exp \Big( \!\! \!\! \sum_{\; \; 1\leq k\leq p} \!\! \!\!  \mathrm{i}\,   \langle y_k , \sqrt{\beta}.R_{u_k} \rangle   \Big) \Big]  \Big| \nonumber  \\
\!\! \!\!  & \leq & \!\! \!\!  p^3 \big( m_1\! +\!  \sqrt{d \lVert \beta \rVert} \big)r + d^2p^4 \big( |u_1| +\ldots + |u_p| \big) r^2 \big( \delta +
\lVert \beta \rVert^2 r^2+ M r^\kappa \big)\, .
\end{eqnarray}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{parasubaddgauge}  Let $\mathbf{S}_n\! = \!  (S_{n,u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, a sequence of $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued BRWs such that the assumptions \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}$)} hold.  
Then, the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] There are $b\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}^*_+$ and $c\! \in \!  [1, \infty)$ such that for all $x,y\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}$, $G(x+y)\! \leq \!  cG(x)e^{b |y|} $.



\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] We set $G_* (x) \! :=\! cG(0)e^{b|x|} $, $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}$, and $\beta_*\! :=\!  2(1+b)^{-2}$. Let $(Z_u)_{u\in \mathbb{U}}$, be i.i.d.~$\mathbb{R}$-valued centered 
r.v.s whose density is ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (dx) \! :=\!  (2\beta_*)^{-1/2} \exp (-|x| (2/\beta_*)^{1/2}) dx$. 
For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and all $u\! \in \!  \mathbf{t}_n\backslash \{ \varnothing\}$, we set $S^*_{n,u} \! = \!  \sum_{v\in \, ]\!] \varnothing , u]\!]} Z_v$. Then $G_*$ is a moment gauge function and the assumptions \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}^*_\cdot, G_*, C_\cdot, \beta_*$)} hold. 





\smallskip


\item[$(iii)$] We assume that the BRWs $(\mathbf S_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued.
