Let $(Y_u)_{u\in \mathbb{U}}$, be i.i.d.~standard $\mathbb{R}$-valued Gaussian r.v.s, which are furthermore assumed to be independent of the $(\mathbf S_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$. For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and all $u\! \in \!  \mathbf{t}_n\backslash \{ \varnothing\}$, we set $S'_{n,u} \! = \!  S_{n,u}+  \sum_{v\in \, ]\!] \varnothing , u]\!]} Y_v $. 
 Then, the assumptions \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}'_\cdot, G, C_\cdot, 1+\boldsymbol{\beta}$)} hold. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{Lukagrowth} We keep the above notations and assumptions. 
We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}. Then the following holds true.
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] Let $c_n \! \in \!  \big(1 \! -\!  \mu_n (1), \infty \big),$ $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, be such that $\lim_{n\to \infty}c_n \! = \!  \infty$. 
For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, we set $a^*_n\! = \!  a_n$, $b^*_n \! = \!  c_nb_n$, $\mu^*_n  (k) \! = \!  c_n^{-1}\mu_n (k)$ for all $k \! \in \!  \mathbb{N}\backslash \{ 1\}$ and $\mu^*_n (1)\! = \!  1\! -\!  c^{-1}_n (1\! -\! \mu_n (1))$. Then, 
the $(\mu^*_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ are non-trivial, critical, they satisfy \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}^*\! , \mathbf{b}^*\! , \boldsymbol \mu^*\! , \psi$)} and for all $z \in [0, a_n]$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{psipsietoi} \psi_n (z) \! :=\!  b_n \big( g_{\mu_n} \big( 1\! -\! \tfrac{z}{a_n}\big)\! -\! 1 + \tfrac{z}{a_n}\big)\! = \!   b^*_n \big( g_{\mu^*_n} \big( 1\! -\! \tfrac{z}{a^*_n}\big)\! -\! 1 + \tfrac{z}{a^*_n}\big)\! =:\! \psi^*_n (z) .
\end{equation}

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] $\limsup_{n\to \infty} (1\! -\! \mu_n (1)) b_n a_n^{-2} \leq 4 \beta_\psi$. 


\smallskip



\item[$(iii)$] If $\psi$ satisfies \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)}, then $\lambda_n\!  := \! b_n / a_n \to \infty$. 

\smallskip



\item[$(iv)$]  For all $y\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, $\lim_{n\to \infty} \max_{\lambda \in [0, y]}|\psi (\lambda) \! -\! \psi_n (\lambda)| \! = \!  0$. 

\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
\label{Lukagrowthrem} By Lemma \ref{Lukagrowth} $(i)$, we see that \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a} , \mathbf{b} , \boldsymbol \mu , \psi$) imposes no limitation on how big $b_n$ can be with respect to $a_n$ but if $b_n a_n^{-2}$ is unbounded, then Lemma \ref{Lukagrowth} $(ii)$ entails that $\limsup_{n\to \infty} 
 \mu_n (1)\! = \!  1$ and $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ can not be seen as the genuine time-renormalization sequence for the RWs $(V^{_{(n)}}_{^k})_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$. However, if $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is the true time-renormalization, namely if $\limsup_{n\to \infty} 
 \mu_n (1) \! < \!  1$,  
then \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a} , \mathbf{b} , \boldsymbol \mu , \psi$) implies that $b_n \! = \!  \mathcal O (a_n^2)$ and furthermore we get $b_na_n^{-2} \! \to \! 0$ if $\beta_\psi \! = \!  0$. We also observe that \texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$) and \texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a} , \mathbf{b} , \boldsymbol \mu , \psi$) actually impose the \emph{minimal} growth condition $b_n / a_n \! \to \! \infty$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{controlgrey}
 Let $\psi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)}. Let 
$(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ and $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Norm} $_{^{\,}}\! $($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$)}. Let $(\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ satisfy (\ref{nontricri}). We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu,\psi$)}. Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$]  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} implies  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)} and  \emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] Let us assume 
\emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}
and $\liminf_{n\to \infty} \mu_n(1) \! > \!  0$. Then \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} is equivalent to  \emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}. 

\item[$(iii)$] Let $G_n$ and $G$ be as in (\ref{defGnG}) and let $v_n$ and $v$ stand for their inverses. We assume  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}. Then $\lim_{n\to \infty} v_n (z) \! = \!  v(z)$ for all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{controlpsi} Let $\psi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Var}$_{\infty}$($\psi$)}. Let 
$(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ and $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Norm} $_{^{\,}\! }$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$)}. Let $(\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ satisfy (\ref{nontricri}). Let $F_n$ and $F$ be as in (\ref{defFnF}) and let $w_n$ and $w$ be their respective inverses. We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu,\psi$)} and \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}.  
Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] $\psi$ satisfies \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}.

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} holds true (and so does 
\emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $)} by Lemma \ref{controlgrey}). 

\smallskip

\item[$(iii)$]  $\lim_{n\to \infty} w_n (z)$ $\! = \! $  $w (z)$ for all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{sheustable} We assume the following: for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, $\mu_n\! = \!  \mu$, $b_n\! = \!  n$ and 
for all $\lambda\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, $\psi(\lambda) \! = \!  \lambda^\alpha$, with $\alpha \! \in \!  (1, 2]$. We also assume that there 
exists a sequence of positive real numbers $(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ tending to $\infty$ such that \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu,\psi$)} holds true. Then \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} holds true too as well as 
\emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} and \emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{Greyexplain} Let $\psi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Var}$_\infty$($\psi$)}. Let $a_n$, $b_n$, $\mu_n$, $\mathbf{t}_n\! = \!  \tau^{_\infty}_n$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and $(C,Y)$ be as in $\emph{\textbf{Case (0)}}$
and let $(\mathcal T_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ and $(\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_s)_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ be as above. 
We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}. Then the following assertions are equivalent. 


\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip


\item[$(a)$]  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}. 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] \emph{\textrm{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}} and $\smash{\tfrac{1}{{\lambda_n}}\Gamma (\mathcal T_n^x)\! \to \! \Gamma_{\! x}}$ in law on $\smash{\mathbb{R}_+}$ (where $\smash{\Gamma_{\! x}}$ is as in (\ref{szhghtLAW})).
                                                                                 
                        

\smallskip



\item[$(c)$]$\lim_{n\to \infty} ( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} )\! = \!  ( C_\cdot , C_\cdot, Y_\cdot)$ in law  in $(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})^2 \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R})$. 

\smallskip


\item[$(d)$] \emph{\textrm{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}}, \emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu $)} $\; $  and $\; $ $b_n / (a_n\log a_n)  \! \to \! \infty $. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
Moreover if  \emph{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} holds true, then the processes $(\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{s}\!\! - C^{_{(n)}}_{s})_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ tend to the null function in probability in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
We set $\mathbf m_{n,x} \! : =\!  \max_{1\leq p\leq \lfloor a_n x\rfloor} \max_{u\in \tau_n (p)} \ell_{n,u}(p) $ and we observe that $\mathbf m_{n,x} \! \leq \!  \Gamma (\mathcal T^x_n) $. 
Then for all $\delta, z \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ we get 
\begin{eqnarray}
{\bf P} \big( \Gamma (\mathcal T^x_n) \! \geq \!  \lambda_n z \big)  \!\! \!\! &\geq &  \!\! \!\!  {\bf P} \big(\mathbf m_{n,x}  \! \geq \!    \lambda_n z  \big) =  {\bf E} \big[1\! -\!  \big(1\! -\! e^{-\lambda_n z} \big)^{\# \tau^x_n} \big]  \nonumber  \\
 \!\! \!\! & \geq &  \!\! \!\! \big( 1\! -\! e^{b_n \delta  \log ( 1 - e^{-\lambda_n z} ) }\big)  {\bf P} \big( \# \tau_n^{_x} \! \geq \!  b_n \delta \big) \nonumber \\
   \!\! \!\! \!& \geq &  \!\! \!\! \!\big( 1\! -\! e^{- \delta e^{\log b_n -\lambda_n z}  }\big) {\bf P} \big( \# \tau_n^{_x} \! \geq \!  b_n \delta \big) \label{extremearg}
\end{eqnarray}
We then recall from (\ref{szhghtGW}) that $\frac{1}{b_n}\# \tau_n^{_x}$ tends to $\varsigma_{-x}$ in law and we also 
recall from $\textbf{Step (B)}$ that \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) implies \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$) and  $\frac{1}{\lambda_n} \Gamma (\mathcal T^x_n)\! \to \! \Gamma_{\! x}$ in law on $\mathbb{R}_+$. Let us suppose that there is an increasing sequence of integers $(n_k)_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$ such that $\lim_{k\to \infty} 
\log b_{n_k} \! -\! z\lambda_{n_k}\! = \!  \infty$. Then the previous convergences combined with (\ref{extremearg}) imply 
$ {\bf P} (\varsigma_{-x} \! \geq \!  \delta) \! \leq \!  {\bf P} (\Gamma_x \! \geq \!  z)\! = \!  1\! -\! e^{-xv(z)} $. Since it holds for arbitrarily small $\delta$ and 
since $\lim_{\delta\to 0^+} {\bf P} (\varsigma_{-x} \! \geq \!  \delta)\! = \!  {\bf P} (\varsigma_{-x} \! > \!  0)\! = \!  1$, we get a contradiction. Thus $\limsup_n (\log b_n \! -\! z\lambda_n) \! < \!  \infty$. This implies that $\limsup_n (\log a_n \! -\! z\lambda_n) \! < \!  \infty$ and 
$\limsup_n (a_n \log a_n)/b_n \! \leq \!  z$. Since it holds for arbitrarily small $z$, we get the desired result. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{fdcvsna} Let $\smash{\beta \! \in \!  \mathrm{Sym}^{+}_{d}}$ and let $\smash{\sqrt{\beta}\!  \in \! \mathrm{Sym}^{+}_{d}}$ be a 
square root of $\beta$, asdiscussed before. Let $C_\cdot$ be a continous nonnegative process such that $C_0\! = \!  0$. We denote by $W$ the (not necessarily continuous) $d$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process 
$C_\cdot$ as in Definition \ref{Brosnadef}. Let $\smash{(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ and $\smash{(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Norm} ($\mathbf a, \mathbf b$)}. For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, let $\smash{\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n, u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}}$ be a $\smash{\mathbb{R}^d}$-valued BRW indexed by a forest of finite trees $\mathbf{t}_n$. We denote by $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ and $\smash{W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot}$ respectively the contour process of $\mathbf{t}_n$ and the snake associated with $\mathbf{S}_n$, which are normalized as in (\ref{renormCW}). 


We assume that $C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! \to \! C_\cdot$ in law in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} $ and that conditionally given $\mathbf{t}_n$, the jumps of $\mathbf{S}_n$ are i.i.d.~r.v.s whose 
deterministic law ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n$ satisfies 
\begin{equation}
\label{bgamdeux}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\!\! y {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy)\! = \!  0 \, , \; \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\!\! |y|^2 {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy) \! < \!  \infty  \; \textrm{and} \; 
\beta_n \! : =\!  \Big( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\!\! y_iy_j {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy) \Big)_{\! \! 1\leq i,j \leq d} \!\!\! \underset{\, n\to\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\,  \beta. 
\end{equation}
Then, for all $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and all nonnegative real numbers $s_p \! \geq \!  \ldots \! \geq \!  s_1 \! \geq \!  0$, the following convergence holds weakly in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \!(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})^{p}$: 
\begin{equation}
\label{cvsnake11bis}
\big( C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot;    W^{_{(n)}}_{\! s_1}, \ldots , W^{_{(n)}}_{\! s_p}\big)  \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;}\big(C_\cdot \, ; \, \sqrt{ \beta} .W_{\! s_1}, \ldots,\sqrt{\beta} .W_{\! s_p} \big)  .
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{fdcvBrosna} Let $C_\cdot$ be a continous nonnegative process such that $C_0\! = \!  0$. We denote by $W$ the (not necessarily continuous) $\mathbb{R}^d$-valued Brownian snake with lifetime process 
$C_\cdot$ as in Definition \ref{Brosnadef}. Let $\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}$ be a sequence of $\mathbb{R}_+$-valued continuous processes such that $\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{0}\! = \!  0$. Conditionally given $\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! $, let $\mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot (\cdot)$ be distributed as the $d$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process 
$\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}$ and initial value the null function. 
We assume that $\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! \to \! C_\cdot$ in law in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} $. 
Then, for all $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and all nonnegative real numbers $s_p \! \geq \!  \ldots \! \geq \!  s_1\! \geq \!  0$, the following convergence holds weakly on $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \!(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^d})^{p}$: 
\begin{equation}
\label{cvbrosnake}
\big(\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot;   \mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\! s_1}, \ldots ,  \mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\! s_p}\big)  \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;}\big(C_\cdot \, ; \,  W_{\! s_1}, \ldots,W_{\! s_p} \big)  
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
In this case, for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ we set $\smash{\mathbf{t}_n\! = \!  \tau^{_\infty}_n\! = \!  (\tau_n (p))_{p\in \mathbb{N}^*}}$, 
which is an infinite GW($\mu_n$)-forest and 
$\smash{\mathcal T_n\! = \!  \big( \mathcal T_n(p)\! = \!  (\tau_n (p), (\ell_{n,u} (p))_{u\in \tau_n (p)} ) \big)_{\! p\in \mathbb{N}^*}}$, 
which is an infinite GW($\mu_n, 1$)-forest, i.e., conditionally given $\smash{\tau^{_\infty}_n}$, the $\smash{\ell_{n,u} (p)}$ 
are independent 
$\mathtt{expo} (1)$ r.v.s. We recall here that $\smash{C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! }$, $\smash{H^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! }$ and $\smash{V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! }$ are resp.~the contour process, the 
height process and the \L{}ukasiewicz path of $\smash{\tau^{_\infty}_n}$, which are normalized as in (\ref{rerescale}) 
(or (\ref{renormCW})). 
$$\emph{\textrm{We overall assume }} \textrm{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)} \emph{\textrm{ and }} 
\textrm{\texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$).} $$
 By Lemma \ref{Lukagrowth} $(iii)$, $\lim_{n\to \infty}\lambda_n\! = \!  \infty$, so \texttt{Norm}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}$) holds true too. Moreover, \texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) holds true, by Lemma \ref{controlgrey} $(i)$, as well as the convergences (\ref{basiccvtreee}) and (\ref{szhghtGW}). 

We denote by $\smash{\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$ the contour process  $\smash{(\mathscr C_{s} (\mathcal T_n))_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}$ of $\mathcal T_n$, which is normalized as in (\ref{rererescale}). Under the previous assumption, Theorem \ref{Greyexplain} asserts that 
\begin{equation} 
\label{CCproba0}
\textrm{ $(\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{s}\!\! - C^{_{(n)}}_{s})_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ tends to the null function in probability in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$.}
\end{equation}

We denote by $(\mathscr W_s(\mathcal T_n, r))_{s, r\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ the contour-Brownian snake of $\mathcal T_n$, i.e., the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process $\mathscr C_{\cdot} (\mathcal T_n)$ as introduced in Definition \ref{1brosnadistree}. 
We recall that $\mathbf{S}_n$ is a BRW indexed by the forest $\tau^{_\infty}_n$ such that, conditionally given $\tau^{_\infty}_n$, the jumps of $\mathbf{S}_n$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued i.i.d.~r.v.s with law $\smash{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (dx)\! = \!  (2\beta)^{-1/2}e^{-(|x|\sqrt{2})/\sqrt{\beta}} dx}$. We first observe that \emph{we only need to prove the $\beta\! = \!  1$ case}, for the general case follows by multiplying the $\beta\! = \!  1$ snakes by $\sqrt{\beta}$. W.l.o.g., we can also assume that {\emph{the BRW $\mathbf S_n$ is coupled with the contour-Brownian snake $(\mathscr W_s(\mathcal T_n, r))_{s, r\in \mathbb{R}_+}$ as in Lemma \ref{compareBrowsnakeBRW}}, which therefore applies (conditionally given $\tau^{_\infty}_n$).  
To simplify, we also set
\begin{equation}
\label{rerererescale}
\forall r,s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+, \quad W_{s}^{_{(n)}} (r) = \tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_n}}W_{b_ns} (\mathbf{S}_n , \lambda_n r) \quad \textrm{and} \quad \mathscr W_{s}^{_{(n)}} (r)=  \tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda_n}}\mathscr W_{b_ns} (\mathcal T_n, \lambda_n r).
\end{equation}
Note that $\smash{(\mathscr W_{\cdot}^{_{(n)}}\! (r))_{r,s\in \mathbb{R}_+}}$ is the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process 
$\smash{\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}}$. 
We recall that $\smash{\widehat{W}_{s}^{_{(n)}}\! \! = \!  W_{s}^{_{(n)}} (C^{_{(n)}}_{s})}$ and 
$\smash{\widehat{\mathscr W}_{s}^{_{(n)}}\! \! = \!   \mathscr W_{s}^{_{(n)}} (\mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_{s})}$ are the endpoint processes 
of $\smash{W_{\cdot}^{_{(n)}}\! }$ and $\smash{\mathscr W_{\cdot}^{_{(n)}}\! }$. 
We also denote by $W$ the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime $C$ which is not assumed, a priori, to be continuous. Let $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We recall that 
$$\varsigma_{n,x} = \frac{1}{b_n} \!\!\! \sum_{\; 1\leq p\leq \lfloor a_n x\rfloor} \!\!\! \!\!\! \#\tau_n(p)\, =\inf \big\{ s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+ : V^{_{(n)}}_s\! = \!  -\lfloor a_n x\rfloor /a_n \big\}\; .$$
We also recall from the proof of Theorem \ref{Greyexplain} that  
$ \mathbf r_{n,x}$ stands for $\frac{1}{b_n}$ times the amount of time needed to complete the contour exploration of 
$\smash{\mathcal T^x_n\! :=\!  (\mathcal T_n(p))_{1\leq p\leq \lfloor a_n x\rfloor}}$. Namely 
\begin{equation}
\label{recallrnx} \mathbf r_{n,x}\! = \!  \frac{1}{b_n}\sum_{1\leq p\leq \lfloor a_n x\rfloor} \sum_{u\in \tau_n(p)} 2\ell_{n,u} (p)\; .
\end{equation} 

The proof proceeds in several steps. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$\textbf{Step (1)}$. \emph{If the laws of the endpoint processes $\smash{(\widehat{\mathscr W}^{_{(n)}}_\cdot)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ are tight in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$, then \emph{\texttt{Sheu} $(\psi)$} holds true, which allows to define a continuous version $W$ of the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process $C$. Moreover the following limit }
\begin{equation}
\label{limiBrosna1}
( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , \mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , \varsigma_{n,x}, \mathbf r_{n,x} )\xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;}( C_\cdot , C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W_\cdot , \varsigma_{-x}, 2 \varsigma_{-x})
\end{equation}
\emph{holds weakly in $(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})^2 \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R}) \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})\! \times \! \mathbb{R}_+^2$, for all $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. }


\noindent
\emph{Proof.} By Proposition \ref{endsnake1} $(i)$, the laws of the whole snakes 
$(\mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$, are tight in $\mathbf{C}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$. 
Thus the laws of $\smash{( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , 
\mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}  )_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$ are tight in $(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})^2 \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R}) 
\! \times\!  \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$. Let us consider a limiting process along a subsequence 
$(n_k)_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$: w.l.o.g.~it can be written $(C_\cdot, C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W')$, by 
Theorem \ref{Greyexplain}. Namely, 
$\smash{\lim_{k\to \infty} (\mathscr C^{_{(n_k)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n_k)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n_k)}}_{\cdot} , \mathscr W^{_{(n_k)}}_{\cdot})}$ 
$\! = \!  $  $(C_\cdot, C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W')$ weakly in the appropriate space. 
Then Lemma \ref{fdcvBrosna} implies that $\smash{W'}$ and 
$W$ have the same finite dimensional marginal laws. Since $W'$ is $\mathbf{C} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$-valued, 
this shows that there is a continuous version of the $1$-dimensional Brownian snake with lifetime process 
$C$. Thus \texttt{Sheu} $(\psi)$ holds true by Proposition \ref{BrosnapsiH} $(i)$. By taking $W$ 
continuous, we easily see that 
$( C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W')$ and $( C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W)$ have the same law. This proves 
existence and uniqueness of weak limits of the laws of the processes 
$( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , 
\mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}  )$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$. Namely, it proves that 
\begin{equation}
\label{limiBrosna1bbis}
( \mathscr C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \! , C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! ,V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} , \mathscr W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot} ) \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;} ( C_\cdot , C_\cdot, Y_\cdot, W_\cdot )
\end{equation}
weakly in $(\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})^2 \! \times \! \mathbf{D} (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R}) \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$. 
The same arguments (see Jacod \& Shiryaev \cite{JaSh02} Proposition 2.11, Chapter VI, Section 2a p.~341) 
used to prove that the weak limit 
$\lim_{n\to \infty} \varsigma_{n,x} \! = \!  \varsigma_{-x}$ holds jointly with (\ref{basiccvtreee}) can be used to prove that it holds jointly with (\ref{limiBrosna1bbis}). Next we recall from $\textbf{Step (A)}$ in the proof of Theorem \ref{Greyexplain} that 
$\mathbf r_{n,x}\! = \!  2L_n (\varsigma_{n,x})$ where the process $L_n (\cdot)$  tends to the identity in probability in 
$\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$. Combined with the previous arguments, it implies (\ref{limiBrosna1}). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- head-3: body_md -->
Standard arguments  entail that (\ref{limiBrosna2}) implies $\textbf{Case (1)}$ for all $x\! \in \!  (0,\infty)$. 
It remains to prove that (\ref{limiBrosna2}) in $\textbf{Case (0)}$ implies (\ref{limiBrosna2}) in $\textbf{Case (2)}$
as a consquence of a result of D.~\& Le Gall \cite{DuLG02} Proposition 2.5.2. 

\smallskip

\noindent
\emph{Proof.} We recall that $\tau^{_\infty}_{n}\! = \!  (\tau_n(p))_{p\in \mathbb{N}}$ is a forest of independent GW($\mu_n$)-trees and we fix $c\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We introduce $\rho_{n,c} \! = \!  \inf\{ s\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+\!  : \! C_s (\tau^{_\infty}_{n})\! \geq \!  c\lambda_n \}$, 
$$\ell_{n,c}\! = \!  \sup \big\{ s\! \in \!  [0,  \rho_{n,c}]\! : \! C_s (\tau^{_\infty}_{n})\! = \!  0 \big\} \quad \textrm{and} \quad r_{n,c}\! = \!  \inf \big\{ s\! \in \!  [ \rho_{n,c}, \infty )\! : \! C_s (\tau^{_\infty}_{n})\! = \!  0 \big\}$$
and we also set $\overline{V}_{\! k}( \tau^{_\infty}_{n}) )\! = \!  V_k (\tau^{_\infty}_{n}))-\inf_{l\in [0, k]}V_l ( \tau^{_\infty}_{n})$, $k\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$. 
Then,  
\begin{eqnarray*}
( \overline{V}_{\!\! (\frac{_{1}}{^{2}}\ell_{n,c} + \cdot) \wedge \frac{_1}{^2}r_{n,c}}( \tau^{_\infty}_{n}) \!\!\!\!\!\!\!\! & ,& \!\!\!\!  \!\!\!\!C_{(\ell_{n,c} + \cdot) \wedge r_{n,c}} (\tau^{_\infty}_{n})  ,  W_{(\ell_{n,c} + \cdot) \wedge r_{n,c}} (\tau^{_\infty}_{n}, \cdot) ) 
\; \textrm{under ${\bf P}$} \\
\!\!\!\! &\overset{\textrm{(law)}}{=} &  (V_\cdot (\tau_n(1)), C_\cdot (\tau_n(1)), W_\cdot (\tau_n(1), \cdot ))  \; \textrm{under ${\bf P}(\, \cdot \,| \,  \Gamma (\tau_n (1))\! \geq \!  c\lambda_n )$,}
\end{eqnarray*}
which is the discrete version of (\ref{selectexcu}).  
Then, we recall from (\ref{sigellerrc}) the defintion of  $(\ell_c, r_c)$.  
The arguments of the proof of Prop.~2.5.2 in D.~\& Le Gall \cite{DuLG02} immediately show that the weak limit 
$(b_n^{-1} \ell_{n,c}, b_n^{-1} r_{n,c}) \! \to \! (\ell_c, r_c)$ holds in law jointly with the convergence (\ref{limiBrosna2}) in $\textbf{Case (0)}$: thanks to the previous arguments and (\ref{selectexcu}), it 
entails (\ref{limiBrosna2}) in $\textbf{Case (2)}$. This completes the proof of Theorem \ref{Sheuexplain}.  \hfill $\blacksquare$
<!-- end head-3 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{fdcvsnakeSibInd} We keep the above notations and suppose that the BRWs $\smash{(\mathbf{S}^{ \mathtt a}_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}}$
 satisfy Assumptions $\mathbf{(2}$\textbf{-}$\mathbf{5)}$ in \emph{\textbf{Sib-Ind}$_{_{\, }}$($\smash{\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \mathbf{S}^{\mathtt a}_\cdot, G_{\! \mathtt a}, C_\cdot, \boldsymbol{\beta}_{\! \mathtt a}}$)}. Then, the finite dimensional marginal laws of the 
 $\smash{\big(C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}\! , \widehat{W}^{_{\mathtt a , (n)}}_{\cdot}\big)}$ weakly converge to those of $\smash{\big( C_\cdot\, , \sqrt{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\! \mathtt a}} . \widehat{W}\big)}$.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{coupleffec} We keep the previous notations and assumptions. Then for all $\varepsilon,\eta \! \in \!  (0, \frac{_1}{^4})$, there are $\theta_\varepsilon \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and $n_{\varepsilon, \eta} \! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ such that $\lim_{\varepsilon \to 0^+} \theta_\varepsilon\! = \!  0$ and such that for all integers $n\! \geq \!  n_{\varepsilon, \eta}$, all $N,p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$, the following holds true. 


\smallskip

\noindent
$\! (i)$ ${\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{1, (n)} ) \! > \!  5\eta \big) \! \leq \!  \theta_\varepsilon +  {\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{0, (n)} ) \! > \!  3\eta \big) \! \leq \!  2\theta_\varepsilon+{\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{1, (n)} ) \! > \!  \eta \big) $.

\smallskip

\noindent
$\! (ii)$ ${\bf P} \big( \sup_{ s\in \mathbb{R}_+}  | \widehat{W}^{1, (n)}_s | \! > \!  z+ \eta \big) \! \leq \!   \theta_\varepsilon + 
{\bf P} \big( \sup_{ s\in \mathbb{R}_+}  | \widehat{W}^{0, (n)}_s |  \! > \!  z \big) $.


\noindent
We set $q_{n,\eta, \varepsilon}\! = \!  \theta_\varepsilon + 4 {\bf P} \big( \sup_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+} | \widehat{W}^{\bullet, (n)}_s|  \! > \!  \tfrac{\eta}{2\varepsilon^{7/2}} \big) + 4e^{-\eta^2/(16\varepsilon^4)}$ and we get the following. 


\noindent
$\! (iii)$ $ q_{n,\eta, \varepsilon} \leq 5\theta_\varepsilon +  16\, {\bf P} \big(\sup_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+} | \widehat{W}^{1, (n)}_s | \! > \!  \tfrac{\eta}{4\varepsilon^3} \big) + 20\, e^{-\frac{\eta^2}{ 16\varepsilon^4}}  $, 

\smallskip

\noindent
$\! (iv)$ $\! {\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{\bullet, (n)} )\!  \! > \! \!  \tfrac{5\eta}{\sqrt{2\varepsilon}} \big) \! \leq \!  2q_{n,\eta, \varepsilon}\! +  {\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{1, (n)} ) \! \! > \! \!  3\eta \big) \! \leq \!   4q_{n,\eta, \varepsilon}\! + $ $ {\bf P} \big( \omega_{N,p} (W^{\bullet, (n)} )\!  \! > \!   \eta\sqrt{2\varepsilon} \big)  $. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(v)$ The laws of $(\widehat{W}^{0, (n)}_\cdot )_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ are tight in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$ iff the same holds true for the laws of $(\widehat{W}^{\bullet, (n)}_\cdot)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$.
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{examplphi} The following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] Let $\phi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{$\texttt{Var}_\infty(\phi)$}. Then $\phi$ is strictly convex, $\phi'$ is concave, $\phi (z) \asymp_\infty z\phi'(z)$, 
$\lim_{z\to \infty}z^{-1} \phi (z)  \! = \!  \infty$, $\lim_{z\to \infty}z^{-2} \phi (z)\! = \!  \beta_\phi$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] For all $(\gamma, c)\! \in \!  \big( \{ 1\} \! \times \! \mathbb{R}_+^*\big) \cup \big( (1,2) \! \times \! \mathbb{R}\big) \cup \big(
 \{2\} \! \times \! (-\infty,0] \big) $ 
there exists $\phi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ such that $\phi (z) \! \asymp_\infty \! z^\gamma (\log z)^c$ 
and there exists $\phi \! \in \!  \mathscr L$ such that $\phi (z) \! \asymp_\infty \! z \log \log z $. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{rigimuphi} Let $\phi \! \in \!  \mathscr{L}$ satisfy \emph{$\texttt{Var}_\infty(\phi)$}, let $a\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and let $q\! \in \!  [0, 1)$. Then there exists an 
offspring distribution $(\mu(k))_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$ such that $ \mu (0)+ \mu (1)\!  \! < \!  1$, $\, \sum_{k\in \mathbb{N}} k\mu(k)\! = \!  1$, 
$\, \mu(1)\! = \!  q$ and for all $r\! \in \!  [0, 1]$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{gmudephi}
g_\mu (r) \! = \!  \sum_{k\in \mathbb{N}} r^k \mu(k) = r+ \frac{1\! -\! q}{a\phi'(a)} \, \phi \big( a(1\! -\! r)\big) \; .
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{muphiregu} Let $\psi \! \in \!  \mathscr{L}$ satisfy \emph{$\texttt{Var}_\infty(\psi)$}. For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, let $F_{\! n} \! \in \!  \mathscr{L} $, 
$a_n \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$, $q_n \! \in \!  [0, 1)$ and let $\mu_n$ be the non-trivial critical offspring distribution such that 
\begin{equation}
\label{Fnmun}
g_{\mu_n} (r) \! = \!  \sum_{k\in \mathbb{N}} r^k \mu_n(k) = r+ \frac{1\! -\! q_n}{a_nF_{\! n}'(a_n)} \, F_{\! n} \big( a_n(1\! -\! r)\big) \; .
\end{equation}  
We also set $b_n \! = \!  a_n F_{\! n}'(a_n) / (1\! -\! q_n)$. Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] For all $z\! \in \!  [0, a_n]$, $\psi_n (z)\! = \!   b_n \big(g_{\mu_n} \big( 1\! -\! \frac{z}{a_n}\big)\! -\! 1+\frac{z}{a_n}\big)\! = \!  F_{\! n} (z)$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] If $a_n \! \to \! \infty$ and if for all $z\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, $\lim_{n\to \infty} F_{\! n} (z)\! = \!  \psi (z)$, then  \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)} holds true. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{sheu1integ} Let $h\! : \! \mathbb{R}_+ \! \to \! \mathbb{R}_+$ be $C^1$, convex, increasing and such that $h(0)\! = \!  0$. Then for all positive real numbers $z_1\! < \!  z_2$, 
\begin{eqnarray}
\label{GreySheunew} 
\int_{z_1}^{z_2} \!\!\!\!  \frac{ds}{sh'(s)} \!\!\! &  \! \leq \!  &  \!\!\!   \int_{z_1}^{z_2} \!\!\! \! \frac{ds}{h(s)} \leq  2\!  \int_{\frac{_1}{^2}z_1}^{\frac{_1}{^2}z_2} \!\!\! \!\! \frac{ds}{\, sh'(s)} \qquad \textrm{and} \; \\
& & \sqrt{2} \! \int_{z_1}^{z_2} \!\!\!\!  \frac{ds}{s\sqrt{h'(s)\,}} \leq  
\int_{z_1}^{z_2} \!\!\! \! \frac{ds}{\sqrt{\int_0^s h(r) dr}} \leq \frac{4}{\sqrt{3}} \int_{\frac{_1}{^4}z_1}^{\frac{_1}{^4}z_2} \!\!\! \!\frac{ds}{\, s\sqrt{h'(s)\, }} \nonumber
\end{eqnarray}
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- head-4: body_md -->
Here we assume that $\phi$ does not satisfy \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\phi$). 
By Lemma \ref{sheu1integ}, we can choose therefore $a'_n$ such that 
$\smash{\int_{a'_{n}}^{a_n} ds/(s\phi'(s)) \! \geq \!  1}$ and we get 
$$ \int_{y}^{a_n} \!\!\! \! \frac{ds}{ \psi_n(s)}  \overset{\textrm{by (\ref{GreySheunew})}}{\geq}  \int_{y}^{a_n} \!\!\! \!\frac{ds}{\, sF_{\! \varepsilon_n, \eta_n}'(s)}  \overset{\textrm{by (\ref{growthF})}}{\geq}  \int_{a'_{n}}^{a_n} \!\!\! \!\frac{ds}{ s\phi'(s)} 
\geq  1 \, , $$
and  \texttt{Grey}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$) is not satisfied. It completes the proof of Proposition \ref{excntrex} $(iii)$.  \hfill $\blacksquare$
<!-- end head-4 -->
<!-- definition-15: body_md -->
\label{GHPpointdef} For $i\! \in \!  \{ 1, 2\}$, let $(E_i, d_i, \rho_i, \mu_i )$ be compact metric spaces where 
$\rho_i \! \in \! E_i$ and $\mu_i\! \in \!  \mathcal{M}_f(E_i)$.
We define their 
\textit{Gromov-Hausdorff-Prokhorov pseudo-distance} by 
\begin{equation}
\label{GHP}
{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}} \big(E_1,   E_2\big) \!  = \!    \inf \! \Big\{  \delta \big( \phi_1 (\rho_1), \phi_2 (\rho_2) \big) \! \vee\!  \delta_{\mathtt{Haus}} (
 \phi_1 (E_1), \phi_2(E_2) ) \! \vee \!  \delta_{\mathtt{Pro}}(  
\mu_1 \! \circ \! \phi_1^{-1}\! ,  \mu_2\!   \circ \! \phi_2^{-1} ) \Big\}, 
\end{equation}
where the infimum is taken on all Polish metric spaces $(X, \delta)$ and all isometrical embeddings $\phi_i \! : \! 
E_i \! \rightarrow \! X$, $i\! \in \!  \{ 1,2\}$; 
here, $ \delta_{\mathtt{Haus}}$ stands for the Hausdorff distance on the space of compact subsets of $X$ and $ \delta_{\mathtt{Pro}}$ stands for
the Prokhorov distance on $\mathcal{M}_{\! f} (X)$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-15 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
\label{Rtreedef} 
A metric space $(T, d)$ is a \emph{$\mathbb{R}$-tree} if it satisfies the following. 

\smallskip

\begin{compactenum}
\item[(a)] For any $\sigma_1, \sigma_2 \! \in\!  T$, there is a unique isometry 
$f:[0,d(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)] \! \rightarrow \! T$ such
that $f(0)\!=\! \sigma_1$ and $f(d(\sigma_1,\sigma_2))\! =\! \sigma_2$. We use the notation 
$[\![ \sigma_1,\sigma_2]\!]_T \! :=\! f([0,d (\sigma_1,\sigma_2)])$ for the geodesic arc. 

\smallskip

\item[(b)] $g([0,1]) \! = \! [\![ g(0),g(1)]\!]_T$, for any  
$g: [0, 1] \! \rightarrow \! T$, continuous and injective. 

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
We denote by $\mathtt{Length}$ the $1$-dimensional Hausdorff measure on $T$, i.e., the (possibly infinite) Borel measure such that $\mathtt{Length}([\![ \sigma_1, \sigma_2]\!]_T)\! = \!  d(\sigma_1, \sigma_2)$, for all $\sigma_1, \sigma_2\! \in \!  T$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{4ptsth} A metric space $(T,d)$ is an $\mathbb{R}$-tree iff it is connected and satisfies the \emph{four points inequality}: 
for all $\sigma_1, \sigma_2, \sigma_3, \sigma_4 \! \in \!  T$,  
\begin{equation}
\label{4ptscondi}
d(\sigma_1 , \sigma_2 ) + d(\sigma_3 , \sigma_4 ) \leq \max \big( d(\sigma_1 , \sigma_3 ) + d(\sigma_2 , \sigma_4 ) 
 \, , \, d(\sigma_1 , \sigma_4 ) + d(\sigma_2 , \sigma_3 ) 
\big) .
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- definition-17: body_md -->
\label{psddef} $(a)$ We denote by $\mathbf{MT}$ the space of $(\zeta, d)\! \in \!  \mathbf{C}^*(\mathbb{R}^2_+ , \mathbb{R})$ such that, for all 
$s_1$, $s_2$, $s_3$, $s_4\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, $d(s_1,s_2)\! = \!   d(s_2,s_1)\! \geq \!  0\! = \!   d(s_1,s_1)$ and 
\begin{equation}
\label{fourpoints}
 d(s_1, s_2) + d(s_3, s_4) \leq \max \big( d(s_1, s_3) + d(s_2, s_4) \, , \,d(s_1, s_4) + d(s_2, s_3) \big) \; .
\end{equation}
We easily check that $\mathbf{MT}$ is a closed subspace of $(\mathbf{C}^*(\mathbb{R}_+^2, \mathbb{R}), \Delta)$ so that $(\mathbf{MT}, \Delta)$ is Polish. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ Let $(\zeta, d)\! \in \!  \mathbf{MT}$. Clearly, $d$ is a pseudo-metric on $\mathbb{R}_+$ and we write $s_1 \! \sim_d\!  s_2$ iff $d(s_1, s_2)\! = \! 0$, which defines an equivalence relation. We set  
$\mathtt{Tree} (\zeta, d)\! :=\! (T,d,r,\mu)$: here, $T$ is the quotient set $\mathbb{R}_+ / \! \!\! \sim_d$, $r\! :=\! \mathtt{proj}_d (0)$, where 
$\smash{\mathtt{proj}_d\! : \! \mathbb{R}_+ \! \rightarrow \! T}$ is the canonical projection, and $\mu$ is the pushforward measure 
of the Lebesgue measure on $[0, \zeta]$, i.e., $\smash{\int_{T} f(x) \, \mu (dx)\! = \! \int_0^\zeta   f(\mathtt{proj}_d (s))\,  ds}$, for any bounded Borel measurable function $f$. 
Observe that $\mathtt{proj}_d(s)\! = \!  r$, for all $s\! \in \!  [\zeta, \infty)$. Thus $\smash{T\! = \!   \mathtt{proj}_d([0, \zeta])}$ and 
by Theorem \ref{4ptsth}, $(T,d)$ is a compact $\mathbb{R}$-tree. We view $r$ as a root and clearly $\mu \! \in \!  \mathcal{M}_f(T)$.  $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-17 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{pseudoGHP} For all $(\zeta, d), (\zeta'\! , d') \! \in \!  \mathbf{MT}$, 
$$1\wedge {\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}}\big(\mathtt{Tree} (\zeta,d),\mathtt{Tree} (\zeta'\! ,d') \big)\leq 3\! \cdot \!  2^{\zeta\vee \zeta'}\!  \Delta \big( (\zeta, d), (\zeta'\! , d')\big)\; .$$
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{submetric} Let $(E,d_E)$ be Polish and let $Z, Z_n$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, be $E$-valued r.v.s.  
Let $(\zeta, \mathbf{d})$, $(\zeta_n, \mathbf{d}_n)$ and $(\zeta_n, \mathbf{d}_n^*)$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, be $\mathbf{MT}$-valued r.v.s. 
We assume the following. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] $(Z_n, (\zeta_n,\mathbf{d}_n)) \! \longrightarrow \! (Z, (\zeta, \mathbf{d}))$ in law on $E\! \times\! \mathbf{MT}$.  

 

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] For all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and for all 
$ s, s^\prime\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, a.s.~$\mathbf{d}_n^*(s, s^\prime) \! \leq \! \mathbf{d}_n(s, s^\prime)$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(c)$] For all $s, s^\prime\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$, $|\mathbf{d}_n^*(s, s^\prime) \! - \! \mathbf{d}_n(s, s^\prime)|\! \longrightarrow \! 0 $ in probability.  


\smallskip

\end{compactenum}

\noindent
Then, $(Z_n, (\zeta_n,\mathbf{d}^*_n),(\zeta_n,\mathbf{d}_n)) \! \longrightarrow \! (Z, (\zeta, \mathbf{d}), (\zeta,\mathbf{d})) $ in law on 
$E\! \times\! \mathbf{MT}^2$.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- definition-18: body_md -->
\label{distsnadef} $(a)$ We set $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*\!:=\! \big\{ (\zeta, (h,w))\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+ \!\! \times \! \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1: h(0)\! = \!  w_0(0)\! = \!  h(s)\! = \!  0, s\! \in \!  [\zeta, \infty) \big\}$ and $D^*_1 ((\zeta, (h,w)), (\zeta'\! , (h'\! ,w')))\! = \!  |\zeta\! -\! \zeta'|+ D_1((h,w),(h'\! ,w'))$. We easily check that $(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*, D_1^*)$ is Polish, its topology being the relative product topology of $\mathbb{R}_+$ and 
$\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1$.   

\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ Let $(\zeta, (h,w))\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*$ and $s,s'\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. We recall the notations $\widehat{w}_s\! = \!  w_s(h(s))$ and 
$m_h(s,s')\! = \!  \min_{r\in [s\wedge s', s\vee s']} h(r)$ and we define the following: 
\begin{equation}
\label{snadistdef}
d_h(s,s')\! := \! h(s)+h(s')-2m_h(s,s') \quad \textrm{and} \quad d_{h,w} (s, s')\! := \! \widehat{w}_{s}+  \widehat{w}_{s'} -2M_{h,w} (s, s')
\end{equation}
where $M_{h,w} (s,s')\! = \!  \big( \min_{r\in [m_h(s,s'), h(s)]} w_s(r) \big)\wedge \big( \min_{r\in [m_h(s,s'), h(s')]} w_{s'}(r)\big)$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-18 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{contsnadist}
 Let $(\zeta, (h,w)), (\zeta_n, (h_n,w_n))\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$. Then the following holds true. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] $(\zeta, d_h) $ and $(\zeta, d_{h,w})$ belong to $\mathbf{MT}$. 


\smallskip



\item[$(ii)$] If $\lim_{n\to 0} D^*_1 \big((\zeta_n, (h_n, w_n)), (\zeta, (h,w)) \big)\! = \! 0$, then 
$\lim_{n\to 0} \Delta \big( (\zeta_n, d_{h_n}), (\zeta, d_h) \big)\! = \!  0$ and  $\lim_{n\to 0} \Delta \big( (\zeta_n, d_{h_n, w_n}), (\zeta, d_{h, w})\big) \! = \! 0$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{scalingbehav} Let us discuss how scaling affects the previous spaces. Let $\lambda, b\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. 


\noindent
$(a)$  For $i\! \in \!  \{ 1, 2\}$, let $(E_i, d_i, \rho_i, \mu_i )$ be compact metric spaces where $\rho_i \! \in \! E_i$ and $\mu_i\! \in \!  \mathcal{M}_f(E_i)$. We set $E^{_{\lambda,b}}_i $ $\! :=\!$ $  (E_i, \frac{1}{\lambda} d_i, \rho_i, \frac{1}{b} \mu_i)$, $i\! \in \!  \{ 1, 2\}$. Then $(\frac{1}{\lambda}\! \wedge\!  \frac{1}{b} ) {\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}}(E_1,E_2)\! \leq \!   {\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}}(E^{_{\lambda,b}}_1,E^{_{\lambda,b}}_2)\! \leq \!  (\frac{1}{\lambda} \! \vee \! \frac{1}{b} ) {\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}}(E_1,E_2)$ (see Remark 4.12 in \cite{DuKhLiTo22} for details). 



\smallskip

\noindent
$(b)$ Let $(\zeta, d)\! \in \!  \mathbf{MT}$. We set $(T,d,r,\mu) \! :=\! \mathtt{Tree} (\zeta, d)$ and $d'\! :=\! \frac{1}{\lambda} d(bs,bs')$, $s, s'\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. Then we easily check that $(\frac{1}{b} \zeta, d')\! \in \!  \mathbf{MT}$ and $ \mathtt{Tree}(\frac{1}{b}\zeta, d')\! = \!  (T,\frac{1}{\lambda}d, r, \frac{1}{b} \mu)$. 

\smallskip

\noindent
$(c)$ Let $(\zeta, (h,w))\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*$ and $l\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We set $h'(s)\! :=\! \frac{1}{l} h(bs)$ and $w'_s(r)\! :=\! \frac{1}{\lambda} w_{bs} (lr)$, $s,r \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$. Then we easily check that $(\frac{1}{b} \zeta, (h',w'))\! \in \!  \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_1^*$ and, for all $s,s'\! \in \! \mathbb{R}_+$, that $d_{h'} (s,s')\! =\!  \frac{1}{l} d_h (bs,bs')$ and $d_{h'\! ,w'}(s,s')\! = \!  \frac{1}{\lambda} d_{h,w} (bs,bs')$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- lemma-24: body_md -->
\label{snadistdomin}
We keep the previous notation. Then, there is a $\mathbb Z$-valued $\mathbf{t}$-indexed BRW 
$ \smash{\mathbf S\! :=\! (S_u)_{u\in \mathbf{t}}}$, whose jumps,  
conditionally given $\mathbf{t}$, are independent with law $\smash{\frac{_{_1}}{^{^2}} \delta_{-1}\! + \frac{_{_1}}{^{^2}} \delta_{1}}$, and such that 
$\smash{(|\Upsilon_{\! u}|)_{u\in \mathbf{t}}}$ $\smash{ \! = \!  (|S_{u}|)_{u\in \mathbf{t}}}$ a.s. We set $\smash{\zeta_\mathbf{t} \! :=\! 2(\# \mathbf{t} \! -\! 1)}$, we recall from Definition \ref{contsnadef} that 
$\smash{C_\cdot (\mathbf{t})}$ and $\smash{W_\cdot (\mathbf{S} , \cdot)}$ stand for resp.~the contour process of $\mathbf{t}$ and the contour snake of $\mathbf{S}$.
Then, a.s.~for all $s$, $s'\! \in \! $  $[0, \zeta_{\mathbf{t}}]$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{snadistdomina}
G_{s,s'}\! :=\! \tfrac12 d_{C(\mathbf{t}), W(\mathbf{S}, \cdot)} (s,s')-  \tfrac12 \, \overline{\! d}_{\mathtt{gr}} ( \overline{\Upsilon}_{\! v(\overline{\mathbf{t}},s)} ,  \overline{\Upsilon}_{\! v(\overline{\mathbf{t}},s')}) \geq 0 , 
\end{equation}
and ${\bf P} (G_{s,s'} \! \geq \!  x) \leq \mathtt b^{2-x}$ for all $x\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+$.
<!-- end lemma-24 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{unifbounded}
Let $\psi \in \mathscr L$ satisfy \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\psi$)}. Let $(a_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$, $(b_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$, $(\mu_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$, $\mathbf{S}_n \! = \!  (S_{n, u})_{u\in \mathbf{t}_n}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, and $(C,Y,W)$ be as in \emph{$\textbf{Case (i)}$}, $0\! \leq \!  \mathbf i \! \leq \!  2$. 
We recall from (\ref{psidisdef}) the definition of $\psi_n$. 
Recall that $C^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}$, $V^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}$ and $W^{_{(n)}}_{\cdot}$ are rescaled as in (\ref{renormCW}). 

\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(a)$] We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)} and 
\emph{\texttt{Hght}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)}.

\smallskip

\item[$(b)$] We assume for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, that conditionally given $\mathbf{t}_n$, the jumps of $\mathbf{S}_n$ are $\mathbb{R}$-valued and i.i.d.~random variables whose deterministic law is denoted by ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n$. We also assume that there are $c, \beta\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ such that for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{cenuni}
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n ([-c,c]) \! = \!  1, \quad 
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\!  y {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy)\! = \!  0 \quad \textrm{and} \quad \beta_n \! :=\! \int_{\mathbb{R}}\! y^2 {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dy) \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\; } \beta.
\end{equation}

\smallskip

\end{compactenum}
Then the following holds true. 

\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} implies that the following convergence 
\begin{equation}
\label{cvsnake1}
\big( C^{_{(n)}}_\cdot, V^{_{(n)}}_\cdot ,  W^{_{(n)}}_\cdot \big)  \xrightarrow[n\to \infty]{\;} (C, Y , \sqrt{\beta}W) .
\end{equation}
holds weakly in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1} \! \times \! \mathbf{D}(\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbb{R}) \! \times \! \mathbf C (\mathbb{R}_+, \mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1})$. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] Suppose that we are in \emph{$\textbf{Case (0)}$}. We also assume for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ 
that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n \! = \!  \frac{1}{2} (\delta_{-1} + \delta_{1})$ and that $\liminf_{n\to \infty} \mu_n (1) \! > \!  0$. Then \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{^{\,}}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)} is equivalent to the tightness in $\mathbf{C}^{_0}_{^1}$ of the laws of $(\widehat{W}^{_{(n)}}_s)_{s\in \mathbb{R}_+}$, $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
\label{rererema} Theorem \ref{unifbounded} $(i)$ holds true in 
$\textbf{Case (3)}$: it is a specific case of 
a theorem due to Janson \& Marckert \cite{JanMar05} when $\alpha\! = \!  2$ and to Marzouk \cite{Mar20} in the 
other stable cases. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- definition-19: body_md -->
\label{exittime} 
Let $(t(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$ and let $ \big(S(p)\! = \!  (S_u(p))_{u\in t(p)}\big)_{1\leq p\leq N}$ be a (possibly infinite) sequence 
of $\mathbb{R}$-valued BRWs as in Definition \ref{forestdef}. We assume that $\# t(p)\! < \!  \infty$, $1\! \leq \!  p\leq N$. Let $S\! = \!  (S_u)_{u\in t}$ be the BRW associated to 
the sequence $(S(p))_{1\leq p\leq N}$as in Remark \ref{contord}. Namely, $S_\varnothing \! = \!  0$, for all $1\! \leq \!  p\leq N$, $t(p)\! = \!  \theta_{[p]} t$ and for all $u\! \in \!  t(p)$, $S_{[p]\ast u}\! = \!  S_u(p)$.    
We denote by $(v_k)$ (resp.~$(u_l)$ the vertices of $t$ listed in increasing contour order (resp.~depth first order) 
Let $y\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$. We define recursively the sequence of times $(\sigma_q (y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ and $({\bf s}_q (y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ by setting $\sigma_0 (y) \! = \!   {\bf s}_0 (y) \! = \!  0$ and 
\begin{eqnarray*}
\sigma_{q+1} (y) \! = \!  \inf \big\{ k \! > \!  \sigma_{q} (y)   
\!\!\!\! \!\!\!  & : & \!\!\!\!  \!\!\!  | S_{v_{k+1}} \!\!  -\! S_{v_{k+1} \wedge v_{\sigma_q (y )+1}}| \! > \!   y \big\} \\
& \textrm{and} &  {\bf s}_{q+1} (y) \! = \!  \inf \big\{ l \! > \!  {\bf s}_{q} (y)   
:  | S_{u_{l+1}} \!\!  -\! S_{u_{l+1} \wedge u_{{\bf s}_q (y )+1}}| \! > \!   y \big\} , 
\end{eqnarray*}
with the convention that $\inf \emptyset \! = \!  \infty$. We call $(\sigma_q (y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ (resp.~$({\bf s}_q (y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$) the \emph{contour} (resp.~\emph{depth-first}) \emph{$y$ oscillation times} of $S$. $\hfill \square$
<!-- end definition-19 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{timeincrease1} We keep the same notations as in Definition \ref{exittime} and we recall notation $(K_l)_{l\in \mathbb{N}}$ from (\ref{Klcontour}). Then, for all $q\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, ${\bf s}_q (y)$ is finite iff $\sigma_q(y)$ is finite and in that case 
$K ({\bf s}_q (y))\! = \!  \sigma_q(y)$.
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- lemma-26: body_md -->
\label{insert} We keep the same notations as in Definition \ref{exittime}. We fix $y_0\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and we assume that
the jumps of $S$ are bounded by $y_0$: $|\xi_u| \! \leq \!  y_0$, $u\! \in \!  t$. 
We fix $k, k^\prime\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ such that $ k\! < \!  k^\prime$, 
and $y, y_1, y_2\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ such that $y \! \geq \!  y_0$ and $y_2 \! > \!  3y + 2y_0 + y_1$. 
We also assume the following. 
\begin{compactenum}
\smallskip


\item[$(a)$] For all $v\! \in \!  [\![ v_{k+1}\!  \wedge\!  v_{k^\prime+1}, v_{k+1} ]\!] $, $|S_{v_{k+1}} \! \! -\! S_v| \! \leq \!  y_1$. 

\smallskip




\smallskip


\item[$(b)$] There exists an integer $j$ such that $k\! < \!   j \! \leq \!  k^\prime$ and  $|S_{v_{k+1}} \! -\! S_{v_{j+1}}| \! > \!  y_2$. 


\end{compactenum}


\smallskip


\noindent
Then, there exists $q \! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$ such that $k\leq \sigma_q (y) \! < \!  \sigma_{q+1} (y) \! < \!  k^\prime  $.
<!-- end lemma-26 -->
<!-- lemma-27: body_md -->
\label{elcontr} Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be the tree associated with a forest of finite trees. 
Let $(v_k)_{k\in \mathbb{N}}$ be its contour exploration. For all $k\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, set $I_{\leq k}\! = \!  \{ v_j\, ; \, j\! \leq \!  k\}$ and 
$I_{\geq k}\! = \!  \{ v_j\, ; \, j\! \geq \!  k\}$ and let $i_1, \ldots, i_4 \! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ be such that $i_1\! \leq \!  i_2\! \leq \!  i_3\! \leq \!  i_4$. 
Then the following holds true.
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip


\item[$(i)$] If $u\! \in \!  [\![ v_{i_1} , v_{i_2} ]\!]$, then there exists $i\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ such that $i_1 \! \leq \!  i\! \leq \!  i_2$ and $u=v_i$. 


\smallskip


\item[$(ii)$]  $[\![ \varnothing , v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_2} ]\!] \! = \!  I_{\leq i_1}\!  \cap I_{\geq i_2}$. 



\smallskip


\item[$(iii)$] $v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}\! = \!  (v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_2})\wedge(v_{i_2} \wedge v_{i_3})$ 



\smallskip


\item[$(iv)$] $v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_2}\! \in \!  [\![ v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}, v_{i_1}]\!]$ and $v_{i_2} \wedge v_{i_3}\! \in \!  [\![ v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}, v_{i_3}]\!]$. 



\smallskip


\item[$(v)$] If $v_{i_3} \wedge v_{i_4} \! \in \!  [\![ v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}, v_{i_3}]\!]$, then 
$v_{i_2} \wedge v_{i_4} \! \in \!  [\![ v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}, v_{i_3} \wedge v_{i_4}]\!]$. 



\smallskip


\item[$(vi)$] If $v_{i_3} \wedge v_{i_4} \! \in \!  [\![ \varnothing , v_{i_1} \wedge v_{i_3}]\!]$, then 
$v_{i_2} \wedge v_{i_4} =  v_{i_3} \wedge v_{i_4}$. 


\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-27 -->
<!-- lemma-28: body_md -->
\label{timeincrease2} Let $\mu$ be a probability on $\mathbb{N}$ such that $\mu(0)+ \mu(1)\! < \!  1$ and $\sum_{k\in \mathbb{N}} k\mu(k)\! = \!  1$. Let $\mathbf{S} (p)\! = \!  (S_u(p))_{u\in \tau(p)}$, $p\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}^*$, be a sequence of i.i.d.~real valued BRWs such that $\tau(p)$ is a GW($\mu$)-tree and conditionally given $\tau (p)$, the jumps of $\mathbf{S}(p)$ are i.i.d.~centered and not a.s.~null. We denote by $\mathbf{S}\! = \!  (S_u)_{u\in \tau}$ the BRW associated with $(\mathbf{S}(p))_{p\in \mathbb{N}^*}$ as in Remark \ref{contord} $\textbf{(b)}$.
Let $y\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and let $({\bf s}_q(y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ the depth-first $y$ oscillation times of $\mathbf{S}$ (see Definition \ref{exittime}). Then, a.s.~${\bf s}_q(y)\! < \!  \infty$ and the r.v.s $({\bf s}_{q+1} (y) \! -\! {\bf s}_q (y))_{q\in \mathbb{N}}$ are i.i.d.
<!-- end lemma-28 -->
<!-- lemma-29: body_md -->
\label{fungenepropp} We keep the above notations and assumptions. 
Then, for all $s\! \in \!  [0, 1\! -\! \mu (0)]$, we set $R_\mu (s)\! = \!  f^{-1}_\mu (s)\! -\! s$, where $f^{-1}_\mu $ stands for the inverse of $f_\mu$. Then $R_\mu \! : \!  [0, 1\! -\! \mu (0)] \! \rightarrow [0, \mu (0)]$ is a strictly convex increasing bijection such that $R^\prime_\mu (0)\! = \!  0$ and 
$R^\prime_\mu (1\! -\! \mu (0)) \! = \!  \tfrac{1}{\mu (1)} \! -\! 1$, this quantity being infinite if $\mu (1)\! = \!  0$.
<!-- end lemma-29 -->
<!-- lemma-30: body_md -->
\label{mainequa} We keep the previous notations and assumptions and we suppose that $S_\varnothing \! = \!  0$. We also assume that a.s.~$\limsup_{n\to \infty} \mathtt{U}_n \! = \!  \infty$, where $(\mathtt{U}_n)_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is a random walk with independent jumps whose law is ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$. 
\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip

\item[$(i)$] 
 For all $a\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$ and all $r\! \in \!  [0, 1]$, 
\begin{equation}
\label{equaZa}
{\bf E} [\mathcal Z_a]\! = \!  1 \quad \textrm{and} \quad R_\mu \big( \varphi_a (r) \big) \! = \!  \int_{\mathbb{R}}  \!\!  {\boldsymbol{\gamma}} ( \mathrm dy) \, \big(\varphi_{a-y} (r) \! -\! \varphi_a (r) \big) \
\end{equation}
where $ R_\mu$ is defined in Lemma \ref{fungenepropp}. 

\smallskip

\item[$(ii)$] The function $a\! \in \!  \mathbb{R} \! \mapsto \! \varphi_a (1)$ is nonincreasing and 
\begin{equation}
\label{monovarphi}
\varphi_a (1) \! \geq \!  \varphi_b (\varphi_{a-b} (1)) , \quad a\! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*, \; b \! \in \!  (0, a] \; .
\end{equation}

\item[$(iii)$] If  
${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\! = \!  \tfrac{_1}{^2} \rho \delta_{-1}\! +\!  (1\! -\! \rho ) \delta_0 \! +\!  \tfrac{_1}{^2}  \rho \delta_1$, 
with $\rho \! \in \!  (0, 1]$, then $(\mathcal Z_{n})_{n\in \mathbb{N}}$ is a critical GW process and 
$\varphi_{n+m} (r)\! = \!  \varphi_n (\varphi_m (r)) $, $m, n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$ and $r\! \in \!  [0, 1]$. 
\end{compactenum}
<!-- end lemma-30 -->
<!-- lemma-31: body_md -->
\label{keylower} We assume that $\mathbf{S}\! = \!  (S_u)_{u\in \tau}$ satisfies \emph{\textbf{(a)}} and \emph{\textbf{(b)}} as in the beginning of the section. We also suppose that $S_\varnothing\! = \!  0$, that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$ is symmetric (i.e.~${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (\!-\mathrm d y) \! = \!  {\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (\mathrm d y)$), that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} ([-1,1])\! = \!  1$ and that $\gamma_{_{1}}$ $ := $ ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (\{ 1\})$ $\! > \! $ $0$. 
Recall the definition of $\Psi_{\! \mu}$ from (\ref{gfpsiR}) and recall from (\ref{defvarphia}) that $\varphi_a (1)\! = \!  {\bf P} \big(  \max_{u\in \tau} S_u \! \geq \!  a \big)$. Then, the following holds true. 

\smallskip



\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(i)$] For all $ a\! \in \!  [2, \infty)$
\end{compactenum}
\begin{equation}
\label{lowerbound} 
 \int_{\varphi_{a}(1)}^{\varphi_{a-2} (1)} \!\!\!\!  \frac{\mathrm d s}{\sqrt{\! \int_0^s  \Psi_{\! \mu} (\gamma_{_{1}} r) \, \mathrm d r}} \, \geq 2\sqrt{\gamma_{_{1}}  } \quad \textrm{and thus} \quad  \int_{\varphi_{a}(1)}^{1}  \!\!  \frac{\mathrm d s}{\sqrt{\! \int_0^s \Psi_{\! \mu} (\gamma_{_{1}} r) \, \mathrm d r }}\,  \geq \tfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{\gamma_{_{1}}   } a\; , 
\end{equation}
\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(ii)$] We assume that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} \! = \!  \tfrac{_1}{^2} (\delta_{-1}+ \delta_1)$. We note that $\varphi^\prime_1 (1) \! = \!  {\bf P} (\mathcal Z_1\! = \!  1)\! \geq \!  \frac{_1}{^2} g'_\mu ( \frac{_1}{^2}) \! > \!  0$, and in particular  $\varphi^\prime_1 (1) \! \geq \!  \mu(1) /2$. 
Then, for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$, 
\end{compactenum}
\begin{equation}
\label{upperbound} 
 \int_{\! \varphi_{n+1} (1)}^{\varphi_{n} (1)} \!\! \frac{ ds }{\sqrt{\int_0^{s} \Psi_{\! \mu} \big( r \big) \mathrm dr\,  }\, } \leq \frac{2 }{\varphi^\prime_1 (1)^{5/2} } \quad \textrm{and thus} \quad  
\int^1_{\! \varphi_{n} (1)} \!\! \frac{ ds }{\sqrt{\int_0^{s} \Psi_{\! \mu} \big( r \big) \mathrm dr \, }\, } \leq  \frac{2 n}{\varphi^\prime_1 (1)^{5/2} }\; , 
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-31 -->
<!-- head-5: body_md -->
If $z\! \in \!  \big( \varphi_{a+1} (1) , \varphi^+_{a-1} (1) \big) $, then there exists $\varepsilon \! \in \!  (0,\tfrac{_1}{^2})$ such that $\varphi_{a+ 1 -\varepsilon} (1) \! < \!  z \! < \!  \varphi_{a-1 +\varepsilon} (1)$. Thus, 
$a\! -\! 1 +\varepsilon \! \leq \!  \beta (z) \! \leq \!  a+ 1 \! -\! \varepsilon$. Since $(\beta (z), \beta (z) +1) \! \subset \! I_1 (z)$,  
we get 
$$ \ell \big( I_1 (z)\! \cap \! [a, a\! + \! 1]  \big) \geq \ell \big((\beta (z), \beta (z) \! +\! 1) \! \cap \! [a, a\! + \! 1]  \big) \geq \varepsilon >0 \; ,$$
which proves the first inclusion of (\ref{supessen}). 

We next assume that $z\! \in \!  [0, \infty)$ is such that $\ell \big( [a, a\! +\!  1]  \cap  I_1 (z) \big) \! > \!  0$. Since $I_1 (z) $ is contained in $[\beta (z), \beta(z) +1]$, we get  $a\! -\! 1 \! < \!  \beta  (z) \! < \!  a\! + \! 1$. Since  $ \varphi^+_{\beta (z) } (1) \! \leq \!  z  \! \leq \!   \varphi_{\beta (z) } (1)$ (this is a simple consequence of the definition of $\beta$), we get $\varphi_{a+1} (1) \! \leq \!  z \! \leq \!  \varphi^+_{a-1 } (1)$. 
It completes the proof of (\ref{supessen}) and, as already mentioned, it also completes the proof of (\ref{Aupbound}) $\hfill \square$
<!-- end head-5 -->
<!-- head-6: body_md -->
Suppose that $z\! \leq \!  \varphi_a (1)$. By the definition (\ref{defbeta}) of $\beta$, $a\! \leq \!  \beta (z)$. Then, by (\ref{Isimplif}) we get $(\beta (z) , \beta (z) +1) \! \subset \! I_1 (z) \cap [a, \infty) \! \subset \! [\beta (z) , \beta (z) +1]$. Thus, $\ell  (I_1 (z) \cap [a, \infty) )\! = \!  1$, which obviously implies (\ref{voolume}). 

  We next assume that $\varphi_a (1) \! < \!  z \! \leq \!  s \! < \!  \varphi^+_{a-1}(1)$. We first get 
$ a\! -\! 1 \! < \!   \beta(z) \! \leq \!  a$ by the definition (\ref{defbeta}) of $\beta$. By (\ref{Isimplif}), 
we get $(a, \beta (z) +1) \! \subset \! I_1 (z) \cap [a, \infty) \! \subset \! [a, \beta (z) +1]$. Similarly, we get  
$(a, \beta (s) +1) \! \subset \! I_1 (s) \cap [a, a+1] \! \subset \! [a, \beta (s) +1]$. Therefore 
$$  \frac{ \ell \big( I_1 (z) \! \cap \!  (a, \infty)  \big)}{\ell \big( I_1 (s) \! \cap \!  [a,a+1]  \big)}= \frac{\beta (z) +1\! -\! a}{\beta (s) +1 \! -\! a } \geq 1 $$
since $\beta$ is nonincreasing. It completes the proof of (\ref{voolume}). $\hfill \square$
<!-- end head-6 -->
<!-- lemma-32: body_md -->
\label{simplbound} We keep the notations and the assumption as above. We suppose that $S_\varnothing\! = \!  0$. 

\begin{compactenum}

\smallskip


\item[$(i)$] Let ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathtt{s}}$ be as in (\ref{bgamdef}) and let $(S^{\mathtt{s}}_u)_{u\in t}$ be a $\mathbb{R}$-valued BRW whose jumps are i.i.d.~with law ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\mathtt{s}}$. Suppose that $S^{\mathtt{s}}_\varnothing \! = \!  0$. Then, 
\end{compactenum}
\begin{eqnarray}
\label{symcontrol}
\forall y_1, y_2 \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*, \qquad \tfrac{_1}{^2}{\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S^{\mathtt{s}}_u|  \! > \!  y_1 \big) \!\!\!\!  &  \! \leq \!  &\!\!\!\! {\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S_u|  \! > \!  y_1 \big) \nonumber \\
\textrm{and} \qquad {\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S_u | \! > \!  2y_1+ y_2 \big) \!\!\!\! & \leq& \!\!\!\! {\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S^{\mathtt{s}}_u|  \! > \!  y_1 \big) +  \min \Big(1\, ,  \tfrac{\beta \Gamma (t)}{y_2^2} \Big) .
\end{eqnarray}
\begin{compactenum}
\item[$(ii)$] Assume that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}} ([-1, 1])\! = \!  1$ and let ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^\bullet $ and ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^\circ$ be as in (\ref{bgamdef}). Let $(S^\bullet_u)_{u\in t}$ and $(S^\circ_u)_{u\in t}$ 
be $\mathbb{R}$-valued BRWs  whose jumps are i.i.d.~with respective laws ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^\bullet$ and ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^\circ$. Suppose that 
$S^\bullet_\varnothing \! = \!  S^\circ_\varnothing \! = \!  0$. 
Then, for all $y_1, y_2,y_3 \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$
\end{compactenum}
\begin{equation}
\label{finpret} 
{\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S_u|  \! > \!  4(y_1+y_2)\! +\! y_3 \big) \! \leq \!  2{\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S^\circ_u | \! > \!  y_1 \big)\! +\!  2{\bf P} \big( \max_{u\in t} |S^\bullet_u|  \! > \!  y_2 \big)\! +\!  \min \Big(1\, ,  \tfrac{\beta \Gamma (t)}{y_3^2} \Big) .
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-32 -->
<!-- lemma-33: body_md -->
\label{egoutploufa} Let ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$ be a centered probability law on $\mathbb{R}$ such that $m_3 \! :=\!  \int_{\mathbb{R}} |y|^3 {\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (\mathrm dy) \! < \!  \infty$.
We also set $\beta \! = \!   \int_{\mathbb{R}} y^2 {\boldsymbol{\gamma}} (\mathrm dy)$ and $\varrho \! = \!  \beta^{3/2}/m_3 \! \in \!  (0, 1]$. 
Let $t\! \in \!  \mathbb{T}$ be finite and let $(S_u)_{u\in t}$ and $(S^o_u)_{u\in t}$ be BRWs such that $S_\varnothing\! = \!  S^o_\varnothing \! = \!  0$ and 
whose independent jumps have respective laws ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}$ and $\tfrac{_1}{^2} (\delta_{-1}+ \delta_1)$. 
 Then, for all $z \! \in \!  [\tfrac{_1}{^8}, \infty) $,
\begin{equation}
\label{bouclerla}
{\bf P} \big(\!  \max_{u\in t} |S_u| \! > \!  \varrho \sqrt{\beta}  z \big) \, \geq \, \frac{1}{8} \Big( 1\! -\!  \frac{4}{ z\varrho^2 }\Big)\,  {\bf P} \big(\!  \max_{u\in t} |S^o_u| \! > \!  16 z \big) 
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-33 -->
<!-- lemma-34: body_md -->
\label{deviation} We keep the above notations. We assume \emph{\texttt{\L{}uka}($\mathbf a , \mathbf b, \boldsymbol \mu, \psi$)},  
\emph{\texttt{Hght}($\mathbf a , \mathbf b, \boldsymbol \mu$)} and \emph{\texttt{Sheu}$_{\,}$($\mathbf{a}, \mathbf{b}, \boldsymbol \mu$)}, which imply \emph{\texttt{Sheu} ($\psi$)} and thus \emph{\texttt{Grey} ($\psi$)}. 
We only assume here that the ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n$ is such that ${\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n([-c,c])\! = \!  1$, $\int_{\mathbb{R}} x{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_n(dx)\! = \!  0$,
for all $n\! \in \!  \mathbb{N}$. Then, for all $x, y_1, y_2 \! \in \!  \mathbb{R}_+^*$, 
\begin{eqnarray}
\limsup_{n\to \infty} {\bf P} \Big( \max_{u\in \tau^{_x}_{^n} } \, \lvert S_{n,u} \rvert \geq c\sqrt{\lambda_n} \big(8y_1+y_2 \big) \Big) 
\leq 8 \Big( 1 \!\!\!\! &- &\!\!\!\! e^{-6xw (y_1/12)} \Big) \nonumber \\
\label{deviaSenn} \!\!\!\! &+ &\!\!\!\! 
{\bf E} \Big[ \min \Big(1, y_2^{-2}\Gamma_{\! x}\Big) \Big].
\end{eqnarray}
where $w$ stands for the inverse of $F$ as defined in (\ref{defFnF}) and where $\Gamma_{\! x}$ is defined in (\ref{szhghtGW}),
<!-- end lemma-34 -->
